Regarding the natural transformations are morphisms of
\mathsf{CRIG}
, the projection
p\colon R\to\mathrm{T}R
sends each
a\in R
to itself; the zero morphism
\mathrm{T}R\to R
sends each
a
to itself and each
\mathrm{d}a
to
0
; the sum morphism
s\colon\mathrm{T}R\to\mathrm{T}_{2}R
sends each
a
to itself and each
\mathrm{d}a
to
\mathrm{d}a\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathrm{d}a
, where
\mathrm{T}_{2}R=\mathrm{T}R\otimes_{R}\mathrm{T}R
; the vertical lift
l\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}R\to\mathrm{T}R
sends each
a
to itself, each
\mathrm{d}a
and each
\mathrm{d}^{\prime}a
to
0
, and
\mathrm{d}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}a
to
\mathrm{d}a
, where
\mathrm{d}^{\prime}x
is the Kähler differential of
x\in\mathrm{T}R
; finally, the canonical flip
c\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}R\to\mathrm{T}^{2}R
sends each
a
to itself,
\mathrm{d}a
to
\mathrm{d}^{\prime}a
,
\mathrm{d}^{\prime}a
to
\mathrm{d}a
, and
\mathrm{d}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}a
to itself.

This tangent structure restricts to the subcategory
\mathsf{CRING}^{\mathsf{op}}
. Moreover,
\mathsf{CRING}^{\mathsf{op}}
has negatives whose negation
n\colon\mathrm{T}R\to\mathrm{T}R
sends each
a
to itself and each
\mathrm{d}a
to
-\mathrm{d}a
.

A morphism of tangent categories consists of a functor between the underlying categories, together with a distributive law between the functor and the respective tangent bundle functors. The distributive law can be lax, colax, an isomorphism, or the identity. Consequently, there are four different flavours of morphisms of tangent categories.

Definition 2.23
.

Given two tangent categories
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, a
lax tangent morphism
consists of a pair
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
formed by a functor
F\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
together with a natural transformation
\alpha\colon F\circ\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F
, called the
lax distributive law
, compatible with the tangent structures as follows:

\star

Additivity. For every
A\in\mathbb{X}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{id}_{FA},\alpha)\colon(FA,F\mathrm{T}A,Fp,Fz,Fs)\to(FA,\mathrm{T}^{\prime}FA,p^{\prime}_{F},z^{\prime}_{F},s^{\prime}_{F})

is an additive bundle morphism;

\star

Compatibility with the vertical lifts:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathrm{T}^{2}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}F\mathrm{T}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}FA}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathrm{T}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}FA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fl}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l^{\prime}F}

\star

Compatibility with the canonical flips:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathrm{T}^{2}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}F\mathrm{T}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}FA}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathrm{T}^{2}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}F\mathrm{T}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}FA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fc}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\alpha}

A
colax tangent morphism
consists of a pair
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\nrightarrow(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
formed by a functor
G\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
together with a natural transformation
\beta\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ G\Rightarrow G\circ\mathrm{T}
which fulfills the duals of the compatibility condition of a lax distributive law. Moreover, the functor
G
preserves each
n
-fold pullback of the projection along itself and the pullback diagram which establishes the universal property of the vertical lift.

Finally, a colax tangent morphism whose distributive law is invertible is called a
strong tangent morphism
and is a
strict tangent morphism
when the distributive law is the identity. For colax tangent morphisms we employ the following notation
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\nrightarrow(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
. Moreover, we identify a strict tangent morphism with its underlying functor. We also adopt the convention to say that a functor between two tangent categories
strongly
(or
strictly
)
preserves
the tangent structures, when such a functor comes equipped with a distributive law which makes it into a strong strict) tangent morphism.

3
Tangent fibrations and indexed tangent categories

Cockett and Cruttwell in
[
CC18
, Section 5]
observed that differential bundles of a tangent category, which are the analogs of vector bundles in the context of differential geometry (see
[
Mac21
]
), can be naturally organized into a fibration. Such a fibration is also compatible with the tangent structure of differential bundles and with the one of the base category. This led to the introduction of the notion of a
tangent fibration
. Cockett and Cruttwell also showed that the fibres of a tangent fibration carry a canonical tangent structure.

We start this section by recalling this definition and this result. We then discuss a first attempt to extend the Grothendieck construction to tangent fibrations using Cockett and Cruttwell’s result. We will show that their procedure of “splitting” the total tangent category into its fibres inevitably loses information about the total tangent structure. We end this section by comparing the notion of internal fibrations in the
2
-category of tangent categories, as introduced by Street in
[
Str74
]
, with the notion of tangent fibrations.

3.1
Tangent fibrations and their fibres

Informally, a tangent fibration is a fibration between two tangent categories, compatible with the tangent structures. To introduce the required compatibility condition, consider first a cloven fibration
\Pi\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
between two categories, each equipped with an endofunctor
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathrm{T}\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
, respectively; suppose also that
\Pi\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime}=\mathrm{T}\circ\Pi
.

On one hand, consider now a morphism
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{X}
and an object
E^{\prime}
over
A^{\prime}
. Since
\Pi\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime}=\mathrm{T}\Pi E^{\prime}=\mathrm{T}A
, there is a cartesian lift
\varphi^{\left(\mathrm{T}f\right)}\colon(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime})\to\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime}
of
\mathrm{T}f
on
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime}
. On the other hand, one can also apply the endofunctor
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
to the cartesian lift
\varphi^{\left(f\right)}\colon f^{\ast}E^{\prime}\to E^{\prime}
of
f
on
E^{\prime}
and obtain
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(f\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(f^{\ast}E^{\prime})\to\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime}
.

Both
\varphi^{\left(\mathrm{T}f\right)}
and
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(f\right)}
are lifts of
\mathrm{T}f
, since
\Pi(\varphi^{\left(\mathrm{T}f\right)})=\Pi(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(f\right)})=f
. By the universality of the cartesian lift
\varphi^{\left(\mathrm{T}f\right)}
, there is a unique morphism
\kappa^{\left(f\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(f^{\ast}E^{\prime})\dashrightarrow(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime})
making the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(f^{\ast}E^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(\mathrm{T}f\right)}}

Requiring the morphism
\kappa^{\left(f\right)}
to be an isomorphism is to ask the tangent bundle functors
(\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}^{\prime})
to form a
1
-morphism of fibrations, as stated in Definition
2.7
. We follow the convention of referring to the morphisms
\kappa^{\left(f\right)}
as the
distributors
of the tangent bundle functors.

The original definition of a tangent fibration was first introduced by Cockett and Cruttwell in
[
CC18
, Definition 5.2]
. Here we specialize this definition for cloven fibrations.

Definition 3.1
.

A
cloven tangent fibration

\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
between two tangent categories
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, respectively called the
total
and the
base
tangent category, consists of a cloven fibration
\Pi\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
whose underlying functor is a strict tangent morphism and for which the tangent bundle functors
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
and
\mathrm{T}
preserve the cartesian lifts, i.e.,
(\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}^{\prime})\colon\Pi\to\Pi
is a
1
-morphism of fibrations.

Remark 3.2
.

In the following, we assume every tangent fibration to be a cloven tangent fibration.

In
[
CC18
, Section 5]
, Cockett and Cruttwell constructed two important examples of tangent fibrations: the tangent fibrations of display bundles and the one of differential bundles. In the next examples, we recall these two constructions. First, we recall a technical concept, introduced by Cockett and Cruttwell in the same paper to ensure the existence of the cartesian lifts. MacAdam reformulated this notion in
[
Mac21
, Definition 1.2.1]
. Here, we adopt MacAdam’s version.

Definition 3.3
.

A
tangent display system
for a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
consists of a family
\mathscr{D}
of morphisms of
\mathbb{X}
for which the following two conditions hold:

\star

Stability under
\mathrm{T}
-pullbacks. The pullback of a morphism in
\mathscr{D}
along any other morphism exists, its universality is preserved by all functors
\mathrm{T}^{m}
, and is contained in
\mathscr{D}
;

\star

Stability under the tangent bundle functor. The tangent bundle functor sends morphisms of
\mathscr{D}
to morphisms of
\mathscr{D}
.

In the following, when the tangent display system
\mathscr{D}
is clear from the context, we refer to the morphisms of
\mathscr{D}
as
tangent display maps
.

In the tangent category of smooth manifolds (see Example
2.20
), submersions constitute a canonical choice of a tangent display system; in the tangent category of affine schemes (see Example
2.22
), one can consider the family of all morphisms as a tangent display system, since the category is finitely complete and the tangent bundle functor is the right adjoint to the (opposite of the) tangent bundle functor of the tangent category of Example
2.21
.

Remark 3.4
.

Recently, Cruttwell and the author of this paper found a characterization for the morphisms of the maximal tangent display system of any given tangent category. Such a characterization has not been published yet, however, it has already been presented at
[
Cru24a
]
,
[
Cru24
]
, and
[
CL24
]
. This notion will appear in a future paper.

Example 3.5
.

Consider a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
equipped with a tangent display system
\mathscr{D}
and let
\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
denote the category whose objects are tangent display maps and morphisms are commutative squares between them. It is straightforward to see that
\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
inherits a tangent structure whose tangent bundle functor is the tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
applied to tangent display maps. The functor
\Pi\colon\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathbb{X}
which sends each tangent display map
q\colon E\to A
to its codomain
A
and each commutative square
(f,g)\colon(q\colon E\to A)\to(q^{\prime}\colon E^{\prime}\to A^{\prime})
to the base morphism
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
, defines a tangent fibration
\Pi\colon\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
(cf.
[
CC18
, Proposition 5.7]
).

Example 3.6
.

Differential bundles, introduced by Cockett and Cruttwell in
[
CC18
]
to capture the analog of a vector bundle in a tangent category (cf.
[
Mac21
]
), can be organized in a tangent fibration. To see this, consider a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
equipped with a tangent display system
\mathscr{D}
. In Example
3.5
we discussed that the codomain functor
\Pi\colon\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
defines a tangent fibration. By restricting
\Pi
to the full subcategory
\mathsf{DBND}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of differential bundles which are also tangent display maps one obtains a new tangent fibration
\Pi\colon\mathsf{DBND}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Moreover, by restricting
\mathsf{DBND}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to only linear morphisms, i.e., morphisms compatible with the vertical lifts (see
[
CC18
, Definition 2.3]
), one also obtains another tangent fibration
\Pi\colon\mathsf{DBND}_{\mathsf{lnr}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. We invite the reader to consult
[
CC18
, Section 5]
for details on these constructions.

Cockett and Cruttwell proved in
[
CC18
, Theorem 5.3]
that the fibres of a cloven tangent fibration inherit a tangent structure from the total tangent category, strongly preserved by the substitution functors.

Let us briefly recall this construction; consider a tangent fibration
\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and an object
A
of
\mathbb{X}
. The first step is to show that each fibre comes equipped with a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}^{\left(A\right)}
so defined:

tangent bundle functor.

The tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\colon\Pi^{-1}(A)\to\Pi^{-1}(A)
is the functor obtained by composing
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\colon\Pi^{-1}(A)\to\Pi^{-1}(\mathrm{T}A)
with the substitution functor
z^{\ast}\colon\Pi^{\left(-1\right)}(\mathrm{T}A)\to\Pi^{-1}(A)
, induced by the zero morphism
z\colon A\to\mathrm{T}A
;

projection.

The projection
p^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\Rightarrow\mathsf{id}_{\Pi^{-1}A}
is defined by

\displaystyle p^{\left(A\right)}\colon z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\left(z\right)}}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E\xrightarrow{p^{\prime}}E

where
\varphi^{\left(z\right)}
is the cartesian lift of the zero morphism of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
p^{\prime}
denotes the projection of
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
;

zero morphism.

The zero morphism
z^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathsf{id}_{\Pi^{-1}A}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}
is the unique morphism
z^{\left(A\right)}\colon E\to z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E
induced by the universality of the cartesian lift
\varphi^{\left(z\right)}
, which makes the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\left(A\right)}}

n
-fold pullback.

The
n
-fold pullback
\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{n}
of the projection
p^{\left(A\right)}
along itself is the functor obtained by composing
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{n}\colon\Pi^{-1}(A)\to\Pi^{-1}(\mathrm{T}_{n}A)
and the substitution functor
z_{n}^{\ast}\colon\Pi^{-1}(\mathrm{T}_{n}A)\to\Pi^{-1}(A)
, where
z_{n}\colon A\to\mathrm{T}_{n}A
denotes
\langle z,\dots,z\rangle
. The
k
-th projection
\pi_{k}^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{n}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}
is the unique morphism induced by the universality of the cartesian lift
\varphi^{\left(z\right)}
, which makes the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z_{n}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{n}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{n}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z_{n}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{k}^{\left(A\right)}}

sum morphism.

The sum morphism
s^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{2}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}
is the unique morphism
s^{\left(A\right)}\colon z^{\ast}_{2}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{2}E\to z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E
induced by the universality of the cartesian lift
\varphi^{\left(z\right)}
, which makes the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z_{2}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{2}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{2}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\left(A\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z_{2}\right)}}

vertical lift.

The vertical lift
l^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\Rightarrow{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}
is the unique morphism induced by the universality of the cartesian lifts
\varphi^{\left(z_{\mathrm{T}}\right)}
and
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(z\right)}
, which makes the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z_{\mathrm{T}}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(z\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l^{\left(A\right)}}

canonical flip.

The canonical flip
c^{\left(A\right)}\colon{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}\Rightarrow{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}
is the unique morphism induced by the universality of the cartesian lifts
\varphi^{\left(z_{\mathrm{T}}\right)}
and
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(z\right)}
, which makes the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z_{\mathrm{T}}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(z\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\left(A\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z_{\mathrm{T}}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(z\right)}}

When
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
has negatives with negation
n^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
, we can also define:

negation.

The negation
n^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}
is the unique morphism induced by the universality of the cartesian lift
\varphi^{\left(z\right)}
, which makes the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n^{\left(A\right)}}

The next step is to equip each substitution functor
f^{\ast}\colon\Pi^{-1}(A^{\prime})\to\Pi^{-1}(A)
induced by a morphism
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{X}
with a suitable distributive law which makes
f^{\ast}
a strong tangent morphism. Consider the morphism

\displaystyle\begin{split}\alpha^{\left(f\right)}\colon&\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}f^{\ast}K=z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(f^{\ast}E^{\prime})\xrightarrow{z^{\ast}\kappa^{\left(f\right)}}z^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}K\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}(\mathrm{T}f\circ z)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}K\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar---\\
&\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}f\circ z=z\circ f}(z\circ f)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}K\xrightarrow{{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}^{-1}}f^{\ast}z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}K=f^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}K\end{split}

(3.1)

where we used the compositor
\mathfrak{I}^{2}
, the distributor
\kappa^{\left(f\right)}
of the tangent bundle functors, and the naturality of
z
.

Remark 3.7
.

To define the distributive law
\alpha^{\left(f\right)}
we make explicit use of the compositor, however, we could have simply written
z^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}K=f^{\ast}z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}K
, since we are using the compositor and its inverse with the naturality of
z
.

Definition 3.8
.

An
indexed tangent category

(\mathbb{X},\mathrm{T};\mathfrak{I})
consists of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, called the
base
tangent category, together with a pseudofunctor
\mathfrak{I}\colon\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}
from the opposite of the category
\mathbb{X}
to the
2
-category of tangent categories, strong tangent morphisms, and natural transformations compatible with the distributive laws. For each morphism
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
of the base tangent category, the corresponding strong tangent morphism
(f^{\ast},\alpha^{\left(f\right)})\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\mathfrak{I}(f)
is called the
substitution tangent morphism
associated with
f
.

Remark 3.9
.

In Definition
3.8
, the tangent structure on the base category seems to be an unnecessary extra structure to carry around. However, such a tangent structure will play a role in reconstructing the base tangent category of the associated tangent fibration in Theorem
3.25
. In particular, the
1
-morphisms of indexed tangent categories we will consider also interact with the base tangent structures.

Remark 3.10
.

Recently, Pronk and Vooys in
[
PV24
]
introduced a notion of
tangent indexing functor
. In our language, such a functor is an indexed tangent category whose base tangent category is the trivial tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathds{1})
over
\mathbb{X}
(see Example
2.19
).

Notation 3.11
.

Given an indexed tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathfrak{I})
, the tangent category
\mathfrak{I}(A)
associated with an object
A
of
\mathbb{X}
is denoted by
(\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)},\mathbb{T}^{\left(A\right)})
and the sustitution tangent morphism associated with a morphism
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{X}
, by
(f^{\ast},\alpha^{\left(f\right)})\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)},\mathbb{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)})\nrightarrow(\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)},\mathbb{T}^{\left(A\right)})
.

ILet us rewrite
[
CC18
, Theorem 5.3]
in our jargon.

Proposition 3.12
.

Every cloven tangent fibration
\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is associated with an indexed tangent category
\mathscr{I}(\Pi)\colon\kern-4.30554pt=(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathfrak{I})
which sends every object
A
of
\mathbb{X}
to the tangent category
(\Pi^{-1}A,\mathbb{T}^{\left(A\right)})
defined over the fibre of
A
and each morphism
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{X}
to the strong tangent morphism
(f^{\ast},\alpha^{\left(f\right)})
whose distributive law was defined in Equation (
3.1
).

Remark 3.13
.

In the construction of the strong distributive law
\alpha^{\left(f\right)}
in Equation (
3.1
) the invertibility of the distributor
\kappa^{\left(f\right)}
required in Definition
3.1
is a sufficient and necessary condition for
(f^{\ast},\alpha^{\left(f\right)})
to be a strong tangent morphism. It is natural to wonder what happens when we drop this requirement and only consider a cloven fibration
\Pi\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
whose underlying functor is a strict tangent morphism
\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
but for which we do not require the tangent bundle functors to preserve the cartesian lifts. In particular, one might hope that the fibres of this weaker version of a tangent fibration will still be tangent categories and that the substitution functors will be only
colax
tangent morphisms.

Unfortunately, in the definition of the vertical lift
l^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\Rightarrow{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}
and of the canonical flip
c^{\left(A\right)}\colon{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}\Rightarrow{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}
of the tangent structure
\mathbb{T}^{\left(A\right)}
over the fibre
\Pi^{\left(-1\right)}(A)
of an object
A
of
\mathbb{X}
, we needed
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(z\right)}
to be cartesian. This is equivalent to assuming the distributor
\kappa^{\left(z\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(z^{\ast}E^{\prime})\to(\mathrm{T}z)^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime})
to be an isomorphism.

In a previous version of this paper, we considered a weaker version of a tangent fibration in which we only assumed the distributor
\kappa^{\left(z\right)}
associated with the zero morphism to be invertible. In particular, for an arbitrary morphism
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
, the corresponding distributor
\kappa^{\left(f\right)}
was not required to be an isomorphism.

This minimal assumption allows one to construct a tangent structure on each fibre and to equip each substitution functor with a
colax
distributive law.

We also considered a stronger version in which every distributor
\kappa^{\left(f\right)}
was considered to be the identity. This makes the substitution functors into strict tangent morphisms between the fibres. In our investigations, we did not find any condition on the distributors for which the substitution functors would carry a lax, not necessarily invertible, distributive law. We decided to maintain the notation
(f^{\ast},\alpha^{\left(f\right)})\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)},\mathbb{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)})\nrightarrow(\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)},\mathbb{T}^{\left(A\right)})
to stress the “
colax nature
” of the substitution tangent morphisms.

So far, we have not found any concrete examples which justify these extensions; therefore, as suggested by the anonymous referees, we decided to omit them in the new version of the paper and only consider the original version due to Cockett and Cruttwell.

Example 3.14
.

In example
3.5
, we showed that the functor
\Pi\colon\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a tangent fibration, therefore, via Proposition
3.12
, we obtain a corresponding indexed tangent category
\mathscr{I}(\Pi)
which sends each object
A
of
\mathbb{X}
to the slice tangent category of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
over
A
. Concretely, the slice tangent category over
A\in\mathbb{X}
is the category of tangent display maps whose codomain is the object
A
, and whose tangent bundle functor sends a tangent display map
q\colon E\to A
to its
vertical bundle

\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}q\colon VE\to A
, that is the pullback of
\mathrm{T}q
along the zero morphism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces VE}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

An important property of the slice tangent category is that the differential objects on this tangent category are equivalent to display differential bundles of the original category (see
[
CC18
, Proposition 5.12]
).

Example 3.15
.

The corresponding indexed tangent category of the tangent fibration described in Example
3.6

\Pi\colon\mathsf{DBND}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
sends each object
A
of
\mathbb{X}
to the tangent category of display differential bundles over
A
.

The next step is to extend the assignment which sends a tangent fibration to the corresponding indexed tangent category to a functor. We first need to introduce morphisms of tangent fibrations and morphisms of indexed tangent categories.

Definition 3.16
.

Consider two tangent fibrations
\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})
and
\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})
. A
lax
(
colax
)
morphism of tangent fibrations

(F,\beta,F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\colon\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\to\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}
consists of a pair of lax (colax) tangent morphisms

\displaystyle(F,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})

\displaystyle(F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime})

for which the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})}

in
\mathsf{TNGCAT}
. Furthermore,
(F,F^{\prime})\colon\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\to\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}
is required to be a morphism of fibrations as in Definition
2.7
.

Moreover, a morphism of tangent fibration
(F,\beta,F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
is:

\star

strong
if
(F,\beta)
and
(F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
are strong tangent morphisms;

\star

strict
if
(F,\beta)
and
(F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
are strict;

\star

strict on the base
whenever the base tangent morphism
(F,\beta)
is a strict tangent morphism.

Notation 3.17
.

Tangent fibrations together with their morphisms can be organized into categories. In particular,
\mathsf{TNGFIB}
denotes the category of tangent fibrations and lax morphisms of tangent fibrations;
\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co}}
denotes the category of tangent fibrations and colax morphisms of tangent fibrations;
\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\cong}
is the subcategory of
\mathsf{TNGFIB}
of tangent fibrations and strong morphisms; finally,
\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{=}
is the subcategory of
\mathsf{TNGFIB}
in which only strict morphisms are considered. We also denote by
\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{strb}}
,
\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}
, and
\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\cong,\mathsf{strb}}
the subcategories of
\mathsf{TNGFIB}
,
\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co}}
, and
\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\cong}
in which the morphisms are strict on the base.

Definition 3.18
.

Consider two indexed tangent categories
\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}
a
lax
(
colax
)
morphism of indexed tangent categories

(F,\beta,F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime},\kappa)\colon\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\to\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}
consists of the following data:

base tangent morphism.

A lax (colax) tangent morphism
(F,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})
, called the
base
tangent morphism;

indexed tangent morphism.

A collection
(F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
of lax (colax) tangent morphisms

\displaystyle(F^{\left(A\right)},\beta^{\left(A\right)})\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)})

from
\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}(A)=(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)})
to
\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}(FA)=(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)})
, indexed by the objects of
\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}
;

distributors.

A collection
\kappa
of natural transformations, called
distributors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\left(A\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Ff)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa^{\left(f\right)}}

indexed by the morphisms
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}
, and making the following coherence diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\left(A\right)}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}f^{\ast}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\left(A\right)}f^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Ff)^{\ast}F^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}F^{\left(A\right)}f^{\ast}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}(Ff)^{\ast}F^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Ff)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA^{\prime}\right)}F^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}\kappa^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})_{F^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}^{\left(Ff\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\left(A\right)}\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa^{\left(f\right)}_{\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\left(A\right)}f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Ff)^{\ast}\beta^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}

where
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(f\right)}
and
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(Ff\right)}
are the distributive laws of
\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}(f)
and
\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}(Ff)
, respectively. For colax indexed tangent morphisms the compatibility is the same but the direction of
\beta^{\left(-\right)}
is reversed.

Moreover, a morphism of indexed tangent categories is:

\star

strong
when the base tangent morphism and each indexed tangent morphism
(F^{\left(A\right)},\beta^{\left(A\right)})
are strong;

\star

strict
when the base tangent morphism and each indexed tangent morphism
(F^{\left(A\right)},\beta^{\left(A\right)})
are strict;

\star

strict on the base
when the base tangent morphism is strict.

Notation 3.19
.

Indexed tangent categories together with their morphisms can be organized into categories. In particular,
\mathsf{INDXTNG}
denotes the category of indexed tangent categories and lax morphisms of indexed tangent categories;
\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{co}}
denotes the category of indexed tangent categories and colax morphisms of indexed tangent categories;
\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\cong}
is the subcategory of
\mathsf{INDXTNG}
of indexed tangent categories and strong morphisms; finally,
\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{=}
is the subcategory of
\mathsf{INDXTNG}
in which only strict morphisms are considered. We also denote by
\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{strb}}
,
\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}
, and
\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\cong,\mathsf{strb}}
the subcategories of
\mathsf{INDXTNG}
,
\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{co}}
, and
\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\cong}
whose morphisms are strict on the base.

Consider two tangent fibrations
\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})
and
\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})
and a lax morphism of tangent fibrations
(F,F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\colon\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\to\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}
and suppose that
(F,F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
is strict on the base. Thanks to Proposition
3.12
,
\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}
and
\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}
define two indexed tangent categories
\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\mathscr{I}(\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\mathscr{I}(\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}
, respectively. The goal is to show that the morphism
(F,F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
defines a lax morphism
\mathfrak{I}(F,F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\colon\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\to\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}
of indexed tangent categories which is also strict on the base. Let us start with the base tangent morphism:

base tangent morphism.

The base tangent morphism is
F\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})
;

To define the indexed tangent morphism, let us start by noticing that
F^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}
maps an object
E
of the fibre over
A
of
\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}
to
F^{\prime}E
. However, we have
\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}F^{\prime}E=F\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}E=FA
, thus
F^{\prime}E
lives in the fibre over
FA
of
\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}
. So, we can restrict
F^{\prime}
to the fibres over
A
and obtain a functor
F^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}\to\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}
, where
\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}=\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{-1}(A)
and
\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}=\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{-1}(FA)
. Let us extend each
F^{\left(A\right)}
to a lax tangent morphism. Consider the following morphism:

\displaystyle\beta^{\left(A\right)}\colon F^{\left(A\right)}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}E=F^{\prime}z_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}E\xrightarrow{\kappa^{\left(z\right)}}(Fz_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})^{\ast}F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}E\xrightarrow{Fz_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}={z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F}}({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}E\xrightarrow{({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\beta^{\prime}}({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}E=\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}F^{\left(A\right)}E

indexed tangent morphism.

The indexed tangent morphism is the collection of lax tangent morphisms
(F^{\left(A\right)},\beta^{\left(A\right)})\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)})
;

distributors.

The distributors are precisely the distributors of
(F,F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
, as in Definition
2.7
.

Remark 3.20
.

To define the lax distributive law
\beta^{\left(A\right)}
we used that
F
is a strict tangent morphism when we replace
Fz_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}
with
(z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})_{F}
. This greatly simplifies the constructions we are going to study in the next section. However, this technical assumption will be dropped in Section
5
where we will show the most general case.

Notice that the compatibility between the distributors and the distributive laws is a consequence of
\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\beta^{\prime}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}
and of the universality of the cartesian lifts. Concretely, this compatibility consists of the commutativity of the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\prime}z_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}f^{\ast}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\prime}z_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}f)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\prime}f^{\ast}z_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Ff)^{\ast}F^{\prime}z_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}f^{\ast}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}F^{\prime}(\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}f)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(F\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}f)^{\ast}z_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Ff)^{\ast}({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}f^{\ast}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}(Ff)^{\ast}F^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}Ff)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Ff)^{\ast}({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}\lambda^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(Ff\right)}F^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Ff)^{\ast}z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\ast}\beta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Ff)^{\ast}\lambda^{\left(z\right)}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{\left(f\right)}z_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\prime}z_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{\left(z\right)}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\beta^{\prime}f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{\left(z\right)}(\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}f)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}F^{\prime}\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{\left(\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}f\right)}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}}

where
\lambda
denotes the distributors of the
1
-morphism
(F,F^{\prime})
and
\kappa
denotes the distributors of the tangent bundle functors.

To prove the commutativity of the bottom rectangular diagram, let us post-compose the two paths of morphisms by
\varphi^{\left(Ff\right)}\varphi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\right)}
, as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}f^{\ast}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}F^{\prime}(\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}f)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}(F\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}f)^{\ast}F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Ff)^{\ast}({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Ff)^{\ast}({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}f^{\ast}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}F^{\prime}\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\lambda^{\left(f\right)}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Ff)^{\ast}({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\beta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(Ff\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z\right)}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\prime}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}f^{\ast}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}f^{\ast}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}(Ff)^{\ast}F^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}Ff)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Ff)^{\ast}({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}f^{\ast}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}f^{\ast}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}(Ff)^{\ast}F^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}Ff)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}F^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}F^{\prime}K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}\lambda^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(Ff\right)}F^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}_{F})^{\ast}\beta^{\prime}f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(Ff\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}Ff\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(Ff\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}F^{\prime}\varphi^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\right)}}

Since
\Pi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\beta^{\prime}
is the identity, from the cartesian property of
\varphi^{\left(Ff\right)}\varphi_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(z_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\right)}
we conclude that these two paths of morphisms are identical.

Proposition 3.21
.

The assignment which sends a cloven tangent fibration
\Pi
to the corresponding indexed tangent category
\mathscr{I}(\Pi)
extends to a pair of functors. In particular, each lax (colax) morphism of tangent fibrations strict on the base is associated with a lax (colax) morphism of indexed tangent categories, also strict on the base:

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\colon\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{strb}}\to\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{strb}}

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\colon\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}\to\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}

These functors restrict to the subcategory of strong and strict morphisms of tangent fibrations which are strict on the base:

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\colon\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\cong,\mathsf{strb}}\to\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\cong,\mathsf{strb}}

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\colon\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{=,\mathsf{strb}}\to\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{=,\mathsf{strb}}

3.2
The reduced Grothendieck construction for tangent fibrations

In the previous section, we extended Cockett and Cruttwell’s result to a functorial assignment which sends a tangent fibration to a corresponding indexed tangent category. Crucially, for a tangent fibration
\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
the tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
of the total tangent category sends an object
E
of the fibre over
A\in\mathbb{X}
, to
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E
, which lives in the fibre over
\mathrm{T}A
. So, in order to define the tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\colon\Pi^{-1}A\to\Pi^{-1}A
we post-composed the total tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
with the substitution functor
z^{\ast}
induced by the zero morphism of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

This process inevitably destroys part of the information of the total tangent bundle functor. To convince the reader of this phenomenon, consider a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
equipped with a display system as in Example
3.5
. This produces a tangent fibration
\Pi\colon\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
whose total tangent category is the tangent category of tangent display maps
E\to A
of
\mathbb{X}
.

As shown in Example
3.14
, the corresponding indexed tangent category
\mathscr{I}(\Pi)
sends each object
A
of
\mathbb{X}
to the slice tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})/A
, whose tangent bundle functor
\mathbb{T}^{\left(A\right)}
sends a tangent display map
q\colon E\to A
to the vertical bundle
\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}q\colon VE\to A
, defined by the pullback diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{VE}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

In general,
VE
is only strictly included in
\mathrm{T}E
. For instance, in the tangent category of Example
2.22
, consider a ring
R
and let us consider its tangent bundle
p\colon\mathrm{T}R\to R
, where
\mathrm{T}R
is the ring
R[\varepsilon]
, with
\varepsilon^{2}=0
, and
p
sends
\varepsilon
to
0
. From the universality of the vertical lift, one finds out that the vertical bundle of
p
consists of the pullback
\mathrm{T}_{2}R=R[\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}]
of
p
along itself, which is the ring of polynomials with coefficients in
R
in two variables
\varepsilon_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
, such that
\varepsilon_{i}\varepsilon_{j}=0
, for
i,j=1,2
.

Contrarily,
\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{T}R)=\mathrm{T}^{2}R
is the ring
R[\varepsilon][\varepsilon^{\prime}]
, generated by two variables
\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, such that
\varepsilon^{2}=\varepsilon^{\prime 2}=0
, but
\varepsilon^{\prime}\varepsilon\neq 0
. The map
R[\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}]\to R[\varepsilon][\varepsilon^{\prime}]
which sends
\varepsilon_{1}
to
\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon_{2}
to
\varepsilon^{\prime}
is precisely the strict inclusion of the vertical bundle into the tangent bundle.

In this section, we partially reconstruct the original tangent fibration starting from the associated indexed tangent category, by constructing a functor of type
\mathscr{F}\colon\mathsf{INDXTNG}\to\mathsf{TNGFIB}
. We show that this functor together with the functor of Proposition
3.21
forms an adjunction, which, however, is not an equivalence of categories.

The main idea is to equip the category of elements
\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathfrak{I})
(see Section
2.1
) of the underlying indexed category
\mathfrak{I}\colon\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}\to\mathsf{CAT}
with a suitable tangent structure, obtained by “gluing” together the tangent structures of the fibres.

tangent bundle functor.

The tangent bundle functor
\hat{\mathrm{T}}\colon\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathfrak{I})\to\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathfrak{I})
sends an object
(A,E)
to
(\mathrm{T}A,p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)
and a morphism
(f,\xi^{\left(f\right)})\colon(A,E)\to(A^{\prime},E^{\prime})
to the morphism

\displaystyle(\mathrm{T}f,\xi^{\left(\mathrm{T}f\right)})\colon(\mathrm{T}A,p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)\to(\mathrm{T}A^{\prime},p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}E^{\prime})

defined by
\mathrm{T}f\colon\mathrm{T}A\to\mathrm{T}A^{\prime}
together with the morphism

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(\mathrm{T}f\right)}

\displaystyle\colon p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\xi^{\left(f\right)}}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}f^{\ast}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}\alpha^{\left(f\right)}}p^{\ast}f^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}(f\circ p)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}E^{\prime}\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar---

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{f\circ p=p\circ\mathrm{T}f}(p\circ\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}^{-1}}(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}E^{\prime}

where
\mathfrak{I}^{2}
denotes the compositor;

projection.

The projection
\hat{p}\colon\hat{\mathrm{T}}\Rightarrow\mathsf{id}_{\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathfrak{I})}
is the pair

\displaystyle(p,\xi^{\left(p\right)})\colon(\mathrm{T}A,p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)\to(A,E)

formed by
p\colon\mathrm{T}A\to A
and by the morphism:

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(p\right)}\colon p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}p^{\left(A\right)}}p^{\ast}E

zero morphism.

The zero morphism
\hat{z}\colon\mathsf{id}_{\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathfrak{I})}\Rightarrow\hat{\mathrm{T}}
is the pair

\displaystyle(z,\xi^{\left(z\right)})\colon(A,E)\to(\mathrm{T}A,p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)

z\colon A\to\mathrm{T}A
and by the morphism:

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(z\right)}\colon E\xrightarrow{z^{\left(A\right)}}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{I}^{0}}\mathsf{id}_{A}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{p\circ z=\mathsf{id}_{A}}(p\circ z)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}z^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E

n
-fold pullback.

The
n
-fold pullback along the projection
p^{\left(A\right)}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathrm{T}}_{n}(A,E)=(\mathrm{T}_{n}A,\pi_{1}^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{n}E)

\displaystyle(\pi_{k},\xi^{\left(\pi_{k}\right)})\colon(\mathrm{T}_{n}A,\pi_{1}^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{n}E)\to(\mathrm{T}A,p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(\pi_{k}\right)}

\displaystyle\colon\pi_{1}^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}^{\ast}p^{\ast}\pi_{k}^{\left(A\right)}}\pi_{1}^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}^{-1}}(p\circ\pi_{1})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar---

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p\circ\pi_{1}=p\circ\pi_{k}}(p\circ\pi_{k})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}\pi_{k}^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E

sum morphism.

The sum morphism
\hat{s}\colon\hat{\mathrm{T}}_{2}\Rightarrow\hat{\mathrm{T}}

\displaystyle(s,\xi^{\left(s\right)})\colon(\mathrm{T}_{2}A,\pi_{1}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{2}E)\to(\mathrm{T}A,p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(s\right)}\colon

\displaystyle\pi_{1}^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{2}E\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}^{\ast}p^{\ast}s^{\left(A\right)}}\pi_{1}^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}^{-1}}(p\circ\pi_{1})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar---

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p\circ s=p\circ\pi_{1}}(p\circ s)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}s^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E

The vertical lift
\hat{l}\colon\hat{\mathrm{T}}\Rightarrow{\hat{\mathrm{T}}}^{2}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\hat{\mathrm{T}}\circ\hat{\mathrm{T}}
is the pair

\displaystyle(l,\xi^{\left(l\right)})\colon(\mathrm{T}A,p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)\to(\mathrm{T}^{2}A,p_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A\right)}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)

formed by

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(l\right)}

\displaystyle\colon p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}l^{\left(A\right)}}p^{\ast}{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}E\xrightarrow{p=p\circ z\circ p=p\circ p_{\mathrm{T}}\circ l}(p\circ p_{\mathrm{T}}\circ l)^{\ast}{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}E\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar---

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}l^{\ast}p_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}p^{\ast}{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}E\xrightarrow{l^{\ast}p^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}\alpha^{\left(p\right)}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}l^{\ast}p^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}\mathrm{T}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A\right)}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E

canonical flip.

The canonical flip
\hat{c}\colon{\hat{\mathrm{T}}}^{2}\Rightarrow{\hat{\mathrm{T}}}^{2}
is the pair

\displaystyle(c,\xi^{\left(c\right)})\colon(\mathrm{T}^{2}A,p_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A\right)}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)\to(\mathrm{T}^{2}A,p_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A\right)}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(c\right)}

\displaystyle\colon p^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}\mathrm{T}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A\right)}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}{\alpha^{\left(p\right)}}^{-1}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}p^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}p^{\ast}{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}E\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}p^{\ast}c^{\left(A\right)}}p^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}p^{\ast}{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}E\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar\mkern-3.0mu\relbar---

Moreover, if
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
has negatives with negation
n\colon\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}
and each
