, then also the tangent category of elements has negatives whose negation is defined as follows:

negation.

The negation
\hat{n}\colon\hat{\mathrm{T}}\Rightarrow\hat{\mathrm{T}}
is the pair

\displaystyle(n,\xi^{\left(n\right)})\colon(\mathrm{T}A,p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)\to(\mathrm{T}A,p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E)

formed by
n\colon\mathrm{T}A\to\mathrm{T}A
and by the morphism:

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(n\right)}

\displaystyle\colon p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}n^{\left(A\right)}}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{p=p\circ n}(p\circ n)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}n^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E

Lemma 3.22
.

The category
\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T},\mathfrak{I})
of elements of an indexed tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T},\mathfrak{I})
comes equipped with a tangent structure:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{T}}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\left(\hat{\mathrm{T}},\hat{p},\hat{z},\hat{s},\hat{l},\hat{c}\right)

Furthermore, if
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and each tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)},\mathbb{T}^{\left(A\right)})
admit negatives for each
A
of
\mathbb{X}
, so does
\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T},\mathfrak{I})
.

Proof.

The equational axioms required for
\hat{\mathbb{T}}
to define a tangent structure are a direct, but tedious, consequence of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and each
(\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)},\mathbb{T}^{\left(A\right)})
being tangent categories. As an example, let us show that the zero morphism
\hat{z}
is a section of the projection
\hat{p}
, that is:

\displaystyle(p,\xi^{\left(p\right)})\circ(z,\xi^{\left(z\right)})=(\mathsf{id}_{A},\xi^{\left(\mathsf{id}_{A}\right)})\colon(A,E)\to(A,E)

For starters, notice that the equation holds for the base components of the morphisms:

\displaystyle p\circ z=\mathsf{id}_{A}

Let us focus on the fibre components. First, let us recall that

\displaystyle(p,\xi^{\left(p\right)})\circ(z,\xi^{\left(z\right)})=(p\circ z,\xi^{\left(p\circ z\right)})

where:

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(p\circ z\right)}\colon E\xrightarrow{\xi^{\left(z\right)}}z^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{z^{\ast}\xi^{\left(p\right)}}z^{\ast}p^{\ast}E\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}(p\circ z)^{\ast}E=\mathsf{id}_{A}^{\ast}E

However, by definition:

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(z\right)}\colon E\xrightarrow{z^{\left(A\right)}}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{I}^{0}}\mathsf{id}_{A}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E=(p\circ z)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}^{-1}}z^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(p\right)}\colon p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}p^{\left(A\right)}}p^{\ast}E

Thus, using that
p^{\left(A\right)}\circ z^{\left(A\right)}=\mathsf{id}_{E}
, it follows straightforwardly that

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(p\circ z\right)}=\mathfrak{I}^{0}

proving the desired equation. One can prove all the other equational axioms in a similar way. The remaining axioms to be proven are the existence of the
n
-fold pullbacks of the projection along itself, together with the universality of the vertical lift. Let us focus on the existence of the
n
-fold pullback of the projection along itself.

let us start by considering an object
(A^{\prime},E^{\prime})
of
\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T},\mathfrak{I})
and a collection morphisms of
\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T},\mathfrak{I})

\displaystyle(f_{k},\xi^{\left(f\right)}_{k})\colon(A^{\prime},E^{\prime})\to\hat{\mathrm{T}}(A,E)

such that, for each
i,j=1{,\dots,}n
:

\displaystyle\hat{p}\circ(f_{i},\xi^{\left(f\right)}_{i})=\hat{p}\circ(f_{j},\xi^{\left(f\right)}_{j})

From the universality of the
n
-fold pullback of
p
along itself, we obtain a morphism
\langle f_{1}{,\dots,}f_{n}\rangle\colon A^{\prime}\dashrightarrow\mathrm{T}_{n}A
of
\mathbb{X}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{n}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle f_{1}{,\dots,}f_{n}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

let us now consider the following diagram in
\mathbb{X}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f_{n}^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(p\circ f_{1})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(p\circ f_{1})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f_{1}^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(p\circ f_{1})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(p\circ f_{1})^{\ast}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi^{\left(f\right)}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi^{\left(f\right)}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{I}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p\circ f_{1})^{\ast}\xi^{\left(\pi_{n}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p\circ f_{1})^{\ast}\xi^{\left(\pi_{1}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p\circ f_{1})^{\ast}p^{\left(A\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{I}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p\circ f_{1})^{\ast}p^{\left(A\right)}}

Since the substitution tangent morphisms are strong, the functor
(p\circ f_{1})^{\ast}
preserves the
n
-fold pullbacks of the projection
p^{\left(A\right)}
with itself. Therefore, we obtain a unique morphism
E^{\prime}\dashrightarrow(p\circ f_{1})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}E
. let us define:

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(\langle f_{1}{,\dots,}f_{n}\rangle\right)}\colon E^{\prime}\dashrightarrow(p\circ f_{1})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{f_{1}=\pi_{1}\circ\langle f_{1}{,\dots,}f_{n}\rangle}(p\circ\pi_{1}\circ\langle f_{1}{,\dots,}f_{n}\rangle)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}\langle f_{1}{,\dots,}f_{n}\rangle^{\ast}(p\circ\pi_{1})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}E

Thus, we obtain a morphism:

\displaystyle\left\langle(f_{1},\xi^{\left(f\right)}_{1}){,\dots,}(f_{n},\xi^{\left(f\right)}_{n})\right\rangle\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\left(\langle f_{1}{,\dots,}f_{n}\rangle,\xi^{\left(\langle f_{1}{,\dots,}f_{n}\rangle\right)}\right)\colon(A^{\prime},E^{\prime})\to\hat{\mathrm{T}}_{n}(A,E)

It is easy to prove that this is the unique morphism satisfying the equation

\displaystyle\hat{\pi}_{k}\circ\left\langle(f_{1},\xi^{\left(f\right)}_{1}){,\dots,}(f_{n},\xi^{\left(f\right)}_{n})\right\rangle=(f_{k},\xi^{\left(f\right)}_{k})

for each
k=1{,\dots,}n
. Finally, to prove the universal property of the vertical lift, first notice that since
p^{\ast}
is a strong tangent morphism, it preserves the universal property of the vertical lift
l^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\to{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}^{2}
of
(\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)},\mathbb{T}^{\left(A\right)})
. From this observation and the universal property of the vertical lift of the base tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathrm{T})
we prove the desired universal property.
∎

Definition 3.23
.

The tangent category defined in Lemma
3.22
, is called the
tangent category of elements
of an indexed tangent category
\mathfrak{I}
.

It is not hard to see that the functor
\mathscr{I}(\Pi)\colon\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathfrak{I})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which sends each object
(A,E)
of
\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathfrak{I})
to
A
defines a cloven tangent fibration. In particular, by construction,
\Pi
strictly preserves the tangent structures. To prove that the tangent bundle functors preserve the cartesian lifts, notice that the cartesian lift of a morphism
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{X}
along
\Pi
is the morphism

\displaystyle\varphi^{\left(f\right)}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=(f,\xi^{\left(f\right)})\colon(A,f^{\ast}E^{\prime})\to(A^{\prime},E^{\prime})

where:

\displaystyle\xi^{f}\colon f^{\ast}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f^{\ast}\mathsf{id}_{E^{\prime}}}f^{\ast}E^{\prime}

From this, one can see that
\hat{\mathrm{T}}(\varphi^{\left(f\right)})
is the morphism
(\mathrm{T}f,\alpha^{\left(f\right)})
, where
\alpha^{\left(f\right)}
is the distributive law of
f^{\ast}
. Since
f^{\ast}
is a strong tangent morphism, it follows directly that the distributor, which coincides with
\alpha^{\left(f\right)}
, is an isomorphism.

The next step is to prove that this assignment extends to a functor
\mathscr{F}\colon\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{strb}}\to\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{strb}}
which forms an adjunction with
\mathscr{I}
of Proposition
3.21
.

Let us start by considering two indexed tangent categories
\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}
and a lax (colax) morphism of indexed tangent categories
(F,F^{\prime},\beta^{\prime},\kappa)\colon\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\to\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}
which is strict on the base. The goal is to define a lax (colax) morphism of tangent fibrations strict on the base, between the corresponding tangent fibrations
\Pi(\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})
and
\Pi(\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})
:

base tangent morphism.

The base tangent morphism is given by
F\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})
;

total tangent morphism.

The lax (colax) tangent morphism between the total categories is given by the functor
\mathsf{EL}(F,F^{\prime})\colon\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\mathfrak{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})\to\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathfrak{I}_{b})
which sends a pair
(A,E
to
(FA,F^{\prime}E)
and a morphism
(f,\xi^{\left(f\right)})\colon(A,E)\to(A^{\prime},E^{\prime})
to the morphism
(Ff,\xi^{\left(Ff\right)})\colon(FA,F^{\prime}E)\to(FA^{\prime},F^{\prime}E^{\prime})
where
\xi^{\left(Ff\right)}
is the morphism:

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(Ff\right)}\colon F^{\prime}E\xrightarrow{F^{\prime}\xi^{\left(f\right)}}F^{\prime}f^{\ast}E\xrightarrow{\kappa^{\left(f\right)}}(Ff)^{\ast}F^{\prime}K

The lax distributive law
\beta^{\prime}\colon\mathsf{EL}(F,F^{\prime})\circ\hat{\mathrm{T}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\Rightarrow\hat{\mathrm{T}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\circ\mathsf{EL}(F,F^{\prime})
is the morphism
(\beta,\xi^{\left(\beta\right)})\colon(F\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}A,F^{\prime}p_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}E)\to(\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}FA,(p_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})_{F}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}F^{\prime}E)
given by
F\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}A=\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}FA
and by the morphism

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(\beta\right)}\colon F^{\prime}p_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{\kappa^{\left(p_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\right)}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}}(Fp_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})^{\ast}F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{(Fp_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})^{\ast}\beta^{\left(A\right)}}(Fp_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}F^{\prime}E=(p_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})_{F}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}F^{\prime}E

where we used that
Fp_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}=(p_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})_{F}
. When the morphism of tangent fibrations is colax, the corresponding colax distributive law of
\mathsf{EL}(F,F^{\prime})
is the morphism
(\beta,\xi^{\left(\beta\right)})\colon(\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}FA,(p_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})_{F}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}F^{\prime}E)\nrightarrow(F\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}A,F^{\prime}p_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}E)
, given by
\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}FA=F\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}A
and by the morphism:

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(\beta\right)}\colon(p_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})_{F}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}F^{\prime}E=(Fp_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\left(FA\right)}F^{\prime}E\xrightarrow{(Fp_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})^{\ast}\beta^{\left(A\right)}}(Fp_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})^{\ast}F^{\prime}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{{\kappa^{\left(p_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\right)}}^{-1}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}}F^{\prime}p_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\left(A\right)}E

Proposition 3.24
.

The assignment which sends an indexed tangent category
\mathfrak{I}
to the corresponding cloven tangent fibration
\Pi(\mathfrak{I})
extends to a pair of functors. In particular, each lax (colax) morphism of indexed tangent categories strict on the base is associated with a lax (colax) morphism of tangent fibrations, also strict on the base:

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}\colon\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{strb}}\to\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{strb}}

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}\colon\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}\to\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}

These functors restrict to the subcategory of strong and strict morphisms of indexed tangent categories which are strict on the base:

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}\colon\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\cong,\mathsf{strb}}\to\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}\colon\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{=,\mathsf{strb}}\to\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}

We can finally state the main theorem of this section.

Theorem 3.25

(reduced Grothendieck construction for tangent categories)
.

The functors of Propositions
3.21
and
3.24
form the following adjunctions:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{strb}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{strb}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

Moreover, these adjunctions restrict to strong and strict morphisms of indexed tangent categories by forming two further adjunctions:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\cong,\mathsf{strb}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\cong,\mathsf{strb}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{=,\mathsf{strb}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{=,\mathsf{strb}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

Finally, in each of these adjunctions, the counit is an isomorphism.

Proof.

First, recall that by the Grothendieck construction, given a tangent fibration
\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
there is an isomorphism of fibrations between the underlying fibration of
\Pi
and the one of
\mathscr{F}(\mathscr{I}(\Pi))
. Similarly, for an indexed tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathfrak{I})
, the underlying indexed category is isomorphic to the one underlying
\mathscr{I}(\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{I}))
. So, we only need to compare the tangent structure of
\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I}(\Pi))
with
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
, and the indexed tangent structure of
\mathscr{I}(\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{I}))
with the one of
\mathfrak{I}
.

Let us start with an indexed tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathfrak{I})
. It is not hard to see that for an object
A\in\mathbb{X}
the tangent category associated with
A
via
\mathscr{I}(\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{I}))
is defined as follows. The category is precisely
\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}=\mathfrak{I}(A)
, since
\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{I})^{-1}A=\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}
. The tangent bundle functor sends an object
E
of
\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}
to
z^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E
, where
\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}
is the tangent bundle functor of
(\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)},\mathbb{T}^{\left(A\right)})=\mathfrak{I}(A)
. However, notice that
z^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E
via the natural isomorphism:

\displaystyle z^{\ast}p^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{{\mathfrak{I}^{2}}^{-1}}(p\circ z)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{p\circ z=\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}}\mathsf{id}^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E\xrightarrow{{\mathfrak{I}^{0}}^{-1}}\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}E

Similarly, a morphism
\psi\colon E\to E^{\prime}
corresponds to a morphism
(\mathsf{id}_{A},\psi)\colon(A,E)\to(A,E^{\prime})
in the tangent category of elements. Thus, the tangent bundle functor of
\mathscr{I}(\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{I}))(A)
sends
\psi
to
z^{\ast}p^{\ast}\psi
. Therefore, the tangent categories
\mathfrak{I}(A)
and
\mathscr{I}(\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{I}))(A)
are isomorphic via a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\circ\mathscr{F}\Rightarrow\mathsf{id}_{\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{strb}}}

which constitutes the counit of the adjunction.

The next step is to define the unit. Let us consider a tangent fibration
\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and let us compare
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
with
\mathsf{EL}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I}(\Pi))
. The objects of the latter are pairs
(A,E)
formed by
A\in\mathbb{X}
and
E\in\Pi^{-1}A
, morphisms are pairs
(f,\xi^{\left(f\right)})\colon(A,E)\to(A^{\prime},E^{\prime})
formed by a morphism
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{X}
together with a morphism
\xi^{\left(f\right)}\colon E\to f^{\ast}E^{\prime}
of
\Pi^{-1}A
, that is
\Pi(\xi^{\left(f\right)})=\mathsf{id}_{A}
. The tangent bundle functor
\hat{\mathrm{T}}
sends a pair
(A,E)
to
(\mathrm{T}A,p^{\ast}z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E)
and a morphism
(f,\xi^{\left(f\right)})\colon(A,E)\to(A^{\prime},E^{\prime})
to
(\mathrm{T}f,\xi^{\left(\mathrm{T}f\right)})
where:

\displaystyle\xi^{\left(\mathrm{T}f\right)}\colon p^{\ast}z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\xi^{\left(f\right)}}p^{\ast}z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}f^{\ast}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}z^{\ast}\kappa^{\left(f\right)}}p^{\ast}z^{\ast}(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime}\cong p^{\ast}f^{\ast}z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime}\cong(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}p^{\ast}z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E^{\prime}

However, since
p
is not invertible, because
z\circ p
is not the identity, the tangent bundle functor
\hat{\mathrm{T}}
is not isomorphic to
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
. We can still define a natural transformation
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\hat{\mathrm{T}}
, induced by the universality of the cartesian lift
\varphi^{\left(p\right)}
and
\varphi^{\left(z\right)}
, as the unique morphism which makes the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p^{\ast}z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(p\right)}z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\left(z\right)}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\prime}}

This natural transformation together with the isomorphism of categories between the category of elements and
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
defines the unit of the adjunction.
∎

Since the base tangent category is preserved by the functors of the adjunctions of Theorem
3.25
, these adjunction restrict to tangent fibrations and indexed tangent categories on a fixed base tangent category.

Corollary 3.26
.

Given a fixed tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, the adjunctions of Theorem
3.25
restricts to the
2
-categories of tangent fibrations and of indexed tangent categories on
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{strb}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{strb}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co},\mathsf{strb}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{\cong,\mathsf{strb}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\cong,\mathsf{strb}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{INDXTNG}_{=,\mathsf{strb}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{=,\mathsf{strb}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bot}

Corollary 3.27
.

When the base tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
has a trivial tangent structure, the adjunctions of Corollary
3.26
become equivalences.

Proof.

In the proof of Theorem
3.25
, we showed that the reason why the unit of the adjunction is not an isomorphism is that, in general,
z\circ p
is not the identity, where
z
and
p
are the zero morphism and the projection of the base tangent category of a tangent fibration. However, when the base tangent structure is trivial, this condition holds, since
p
and
z
are the identities.
∎

3.3
Tangent fibrations as pseudoalgebras

In
[
Str74
]
, Street presented (cloven) fibrations as pseudoalgebras of a
2
-monad and he employed this characterization to extend the notion of a fibration in the context of
2
-category theory. Since tangent categories form a
2
-category, it is natural to wonder whether or not Street’s definition of fibrations applied to the context of tangent categories provides the same notion of a tangent fibration of Definition
3.1
.

This section is dedicated to exploring this question. Let us start by recalling Street’s construction. Consider the
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories and let
\mathbb{X}
be a fixed category. On the slice
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}/\mathbb{X}
, one can define a
2
-functor
M
which sends a functor
\Pi\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
to
M(\Pi)\colon\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi\to\mathbb{X}
, where
\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi
denotes the comma category of
\Pi
and the functor
M(\Pi)
sends each
(f\colon A\to\Pi(E))\in\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi
to
A\in\mathbb{X}
.

M\colon\mathsf{CAT}/\mathbb{X}\to\mathsf{CAT}/\mathbb{X}
comes equipped with a
2
-monad structure and cloven fibrations are precisely pseudoalgebras of
M
. Let us recall the notion of a pseudoalgebra for a
2
-monad.

Definition 3.28
.

A
pseudoalgebra
of a
2
-monad
M\colon{\mathbb{K}}\to{\mathbb{K}}
consists of an object
A
of
{\mathbb{K}}
together with a
1
-morphism
c\colon MA\to A
of
{\mathbb{K}}
and two
2
-morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces MA}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{2}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces MA}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces MA}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Mc}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

where
\eta\colon\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{K}}\Rightarrow M
and
\gamma\colon M^{2}\Rightarrow M
are the unit and the multiplication of the
2
-monad
M
, respectively. Moreover,
\zeta
and
\theta
satisfy some coherence conditions with the monad structure (see
[
Str74
, Section 2]
).

Concretely, a pseudoalgebra of the
2
-monad
M\colon\mathsf{CAT}/\mathbb{X}\to\mathsf{CAT}/\mathbb{X}
is a functor
\Pi\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
together with a
2
-functor
\mathsf{trans}\colon\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
which sends each
f\colon A\to\Pi(E)
to an object
f^{\ast}E
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
. Moreover, the
2
-functor
\mathsf{trans}
sends the square diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi(E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi(E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi(E)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi(\mathsf{id}_{E})}

to a morphism
f^{\ast}E\to E
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
, which corresponds to the cartesian lift of
f\colon A\to\Pi(E)
on
E
. Finally, the
2
-morphisms
\zeta
and
\theta
provide the unitor and the compositor.

For an arbitrary
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
, the
2
-monad
M
is replaced with the
2
-monad which sends each object
\Pi\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
of the slice
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}/\mathbb{X}
to
M(\Pi)\colon\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi\to\mathbb{X}
, where
\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi
denotes the comma object of
\Pi
, provided the existence of such comma objects.

To see whether or not pseudoalgebras of the
2
-monad
M
in the
2
-category of tangent categories correspond to the notion of a tangent fibration of Definition
3.1
, first we need to establish if such a
2
-category has comma objects. In particular, we need to see whether or not the comma category
\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi
carries a tangent structure when
(\Pi,\alpha)
is a tangent morphism. In general, this is not the case, however, by considering a subclass of tangent morphisms we obtain the following characterization.

Lemma 3.29
.

Let
(\Pi,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
be a strong tangent morphism. Then, the comma category
\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi
carries a tangent structure whose tangent bundle functor sends each
f\colon A\to\Pi(E)
to:

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}f}\mathrm{T}\Pi(E)\xrightarrow{\alpha^{-1}}\Pi\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(E)

Moreover, this tangent category is the comma object of the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}
of tangent categories and strong tangent morphisms which preserve the
n
-fold pullbacks of the projection and the universality of the vertical lift.

Proof.

Let us briefly sketch the proof. To show that the comma category
\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi
comes equipped with a tangent structure, one uses the compatibilities of the strong distributive law
\alpha
with the tangent structures and that the functor
\Pi
preserves the
n
-fold pullbacks of the projection along itself and the universality of the vertical lift.

To prove that such a construction defines comma objects, let us consider another tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
together with two strong tangent morphisms
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and a tangent natural transformation:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Pi,\alpha)}

Let us consider the functor

\displaystyle K\colon

\displaystyle\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi

\displaystyle A\mapsto(\varphi_{A}\colon GA\to\Pi(HA))

\displaystyle(f\colon A\to B)\mapsto((\Pi(Hf),Gf)\colon\varphi_{A}\to\varphi_{B})

together with the distributive law
\delta\colon K\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\bar{\mathrm{T}}\circ K
, so defined:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}GA}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}\Pi HA}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi H\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi\mathrm{T}^{\prime}HA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}\varphi_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}KA}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{-1}H}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

One can prove that this strong tangent morphism preserves the
n
-fold pullbacks of the projection along itself, the universality of the vertical lift, and that it is the unique strong tangent morphism such that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\downarrow(\Pi,\alpha)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K,\delta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\downarrow(\Pi,\alpha)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K,\delta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

where
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
denotes the projections of the comma object
\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi
, which are strict tangent morphisms, and such that:

\displaystyle\varphi=\psi_{(K,\delta)}

where:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\downarrow(\Pi,\alpha)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Pi,\alpha)}

is the tangent natural transformation which picks out the object of the comma category.
∎

Thanks to Lemma
3.29
, we can define the
2
-monad
M\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}/(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}/(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which sends a strong tangent morphism
(\Pi,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to
M(\Pi,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\downarrow(\Pi,\alpha)\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Theorem 3.30
.

Tangent fibrations on a given tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
are precisely pseudoalgebras of the
2
-monad
M\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}/(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}/(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
whose underlying tangent morphism is strict.

Proof.

A pseudoalgebra of
M
consists of a strong tangent morphism
(\Pi,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
together with another strong tangent morphism
(\mathsf{trans},\kappa)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\downarrow(\Pi,\alpha)\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and two natural transformations
\zeta
and
\theta
. In particular, following the same argument used above for when the ambient
2
-category is
\mathsf{CAT}
,
\Pi\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
together with the functor
\mathsf{trans}\colon\mathbb{X}\downarrow\Pi\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
defines a cloven fibration. Moreover, the strong distributive law
\kappa\colon\mathsf{trans}\circ\bar{\mathrm{T}}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ\mathsf{trans}
defines the distributors of the tangent bundle functors, where
\bar{\mathrm{T}}
denotes the tangent bundle functor of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\downarrow(\Pi,\alpha)
.
∎

There is only one important difference between tangent fibrations and pseudoalgebras of
M
: in the latter, the underlying tangent morphism
(\Pi,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T})^{\prime}\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is strong but not necessarily strict. One could have tried to consider the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{=}
of tangent categories and strict tangent morphism instead of
\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}
. However, in this context, the corresponding pseudoalgebras are instead fibrations between tangent categories whose tangent bundle functors strictly (not strongly) preserve the cartesian lifts.

Remark 3.31
.

The notion of an internal fibration of the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}
suggests to investigate a weaker version of the notion of a tangent fibration, whose underlying functor only preserves strongly (and not strictly) the tangent structures. In future work, we are interested in exploring this definition and looking for examples.

4
Tangent objects: a formal approach to tangent categories

Theorem
3.25
provides an adjunction between tangent fibrations and indexed tangent categories; however, the unit of this adjunction is not, in general, an isomorphism. The issue is due to the loss of information caused by pulling back along the zero morphism in order to reposition the tangent bundle functor into the right fibre.

In order to prove a genuine Grothendieck equivalence, we should avoid pulling back along the zero morphism; this would yield a collection of functors of type
\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\colon\Pi^{-1}(A)\to\Pi^{-1}(\mathrm{T}A)
indexed by the objects of the base category
\mathbb{X}
. This intuition suggests looking at a “global tangent structure” over the entire indexed category, instead of a “local tangent structure” on each fibre.

In this section, we want to make precise the idea of a
tangent structure
for indexed categories. Our approach is far more general: we aim to introduce the notion of a tangent structure on
any
object of a given strict
2
-category. This approach is inspired by the formal theory of Street presented in
[
Str72
]
, in which the formal theory of monads on a given
2
-category was explored. Here, we focus our efforts on introducing the main concept; in future work, we intend to explore further this idea.

Leung in his Ph.D. thesis
[
Leu17
]
proposed a simple and effective classification of the tangent structures for a given category
\mathbb{X}
. In particular, he showed that tangent structures
\mathbb{T}
for
\mathbb{X}
are in one-to-one correspondence with strict monoidal functors:

\displaystyle\mathrm{L}[\mathbb{T}]\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\to\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})

from (a subcategory of) the monoidal category of Weil algebras to the monoidal category
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})\colon\kern-4.30554pt={\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X})
of endofunctors over the category
\mathbb{X}
, satisfying extra conditions. A Weil algebra is a commutative and unital
\mathbb{N}
-algebra, obtained by quotienting the
\mathbb{N}
-algebra
\mathbb{N}[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]
of
\mathbb{N}
-polynomials in
n
variables by an ideal generated by monomials of order
2
. More precisely,
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}
denotes the monoidal category generated by the Weil algebras
W^{n}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\mathbb{N}[x_{1}{,\dots,}x_{n}]/(x_{i}x_{j},i\leq j)
, for positive integers
n
. As shown by Leung, the category
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}
comes equipped with a tangent structure whose tangent bundle functor sends a Weil algebra
A
to
A\otimes W
, where
W\colon\kern-4.30554pt=W^{1}
and whose natural transformations are induced by the following morphisms:

projection.

The projection
p\colon W\to\mathbb{N}
, which sends the generator
x
of
W
to
0
;

zero morphism.

The zero morphism
z\colon\mathbb{N}\to W
, which sends an integer to itself;

sum morphism.

The sum morphism
s\colon W^{2}\to W
, which sends the two generators
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
to the unique generator
x
;

vertical lift.

The vertical lift
l\colon W\to W\otimes W
, which sends the generator
x
to
x\otimes y
;

canonical flip.

The canonical flip
c\colon W\otimes W\to W\otimes W
, which sends the generator
x
of the left
W
to the generator
y
of the right
W
, and vice versa, i.e.,
y
to
x
.

Leung’s classification establishes that a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}
over a category
\mathbb{X}
is precisely given by a strict monoidal functor
\mathrm{L}[\mathbb{T}]
which sends the generators
W^{n}
of
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}
to the functors
\mathrm{T}_{n}
, and the morphisms listed above to the homonymous natural transformations of
\mathbb{T}
. In particular, the tangent bundle functor is
\mathrm{T}=\mathrm{L}[\mathbb{T}](W)
, the double tangent bundle functor is
\mathrm{T}^{2}=\mathrm{L}[\mathbb{T}](W\otimes W)
, the projection is
p=\mathrm{L}[\mathbb{T}](p)\colon\mathrm{T}=\mathrm{L}[\mathbb{T}](W)\to\mathrm{L}[\mathbb{T}](\mathbb{N})=\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}
, etcetera.

In this section we explore a generalization of this classification which leads to a simple but important tool for our discussion: the concept of a tangent object.

Let
{\mathbb{K}}
be a fixed strict
2
-category, that is a category enriched over
\mathsf{CAT}
. In future work, we would like to explore weaker versions of this concept, but for now, let us focus on the strict case.

Before defining a tangent object, we first need to introduce an important technical definition.

Definition 4.1
.

Given a strict
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
and two objects
\mathbb{X}
and
\mathbb{Y}
of
{\mathbb{K}}
, a limit in the category
{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})
is
pointwise
when it is preserved by all functors
{\mathbb{K}}(f,\mathbb{Y})\colon{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})\to{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Y})
for every
1
-morphism
f\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
in
{\mathbb{K}}
.

Remark 4.2
.

We would like to thank Rory Lucyshyn-Wright for suggesting this assumption and pointing out its importance for tangent objects in an informal discussion with the author. This aspect was missing in the original definition provided by the author.

When the
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
is the
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories, pointwise limits of
{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})
are those limit diagrams in the category of functors of type
F\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{Y}
that are preserved by the evaluation functor. Concretely, this means that, for an object
X
of
\mathbb{X}
, and a diagram
D\colon\mathbb{X}_{0}\to{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})
, the functor
\lim D\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{Y}
evaluated at
X
is isomorphic to the object
\lim D(X)
of
\mathbb{Y}
, where
D(X)
represents the diagram
\mathbb{X}_{0}\to\mathbb{Y}
obtained by evaluating each functor
D_{A}\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{Y}
, corresponding to each
A
of
\mathbb{X}_{0}
, at
X
.

When the target category
\mathbb{Y}
has all finite limits, then so does the category of functors
{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})
and each limit is pointwise. However, when the target category is not known to be finitely complete, there is no guarantee that the limits of
{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})
will be pointwise. A counterexample can be found in
[
Kel05
, Section 3.3]
.

Unfortunately, in tangent category theory, the requirement of the existence of limits is a subtle matter since in differential geometry not every pair of morphisms admits a pullback. In particular, a tangent category cannot be required to be finitely complete since this would rule out one of the main examples of a tangent category. Consequently, in order to make our definition of tangent objects compatible with the usual notion of a tangent category when
{\mathbb{K}}
is assumed to be the
2
-category of categories, we need to require the limits involved in the definition of a tangent object to be pointwise.

Definition 4.3
.

A
tangent object
in a
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
is an object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbb{K}}
equipped with a
tangent structure

\mathbb{T}
, which consists of a
Leung monoidal functor
, which is a strict monoidal functor
\mathrm{L}[\mathbb{T}]\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\to\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
from the monoidal category of Weil algebras to the monoidal category of endomorphisms over
\mathbb{X}
in
{\mathbb{K}}
, satisfying the following two universal conditions:

\star

\mathrm{L}[\mathbb{T}]
preserves the
foundational pullbacks
, which are pullbacks of the form:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A\otimes(B\times C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A\otimes C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A\otimes B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

for all
A,B,C\in\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}
(cf.
[
Leu17
, Definition 3.17]
), where, for the sake of simplicity, we omitted the unitors in the diagram. Moreover, these pullbacks are pointwise limits;

\star

\mathrm{L}[\mathbb{T}]
preserves the universality of the vertical lift, i.e., the pullback diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W\otimes W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\otimes p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

where
\phi\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\langle z\otimes W,l\rangle(W\otimes s)
and
\pi_{1}\colon W^{2}\to W
sends
x_{1}
to
x
and
x_{2}
to zero, where, for the sake of simplicity, we omitted the unitors in the diagram. Moreover, this pullback is a pointwise limit.

Remark 4.4
.

In Leung’s original result, the universality of the vertical lift of Definition
4.3
, is replaced with the universality of an equalizer. However, Cockett and Cruttwell proved in
[
CC14
, Lemma 2.12]
that the universality of the pullback diagram of Definition
4.3
is equivalent to the universality of the equalizer diagram proposed by Leung. To stay consistent with the rest of the paper, we adopted the pullback version.

Remark 4.5
.

To classify tangent structures with negatives one can replace the rig
\mathbb{N}
with the ring
\mathbb{Z}
in the definition of a Weil algebra and then introduce the negation as follows:

negation.

The negation
n\colon W\to W
sends the generator
x
to
-x
.

Thus, Leung’s classification extends as follows: tangent structures with negatives
\mathbb{T}
over a category
\mathbb{X}
are in one-to-one correspondence with strict monoidal functors
\mathrm{L}^{-}[\mathbb{T}]\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}^{-}\to\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
preserving foundational pullbacks and the universality of the vertical lift, where
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}^{-}
is the category of Weil algebras over the ring
\mathbb{Z}
.

Thanks to Remark
4.5
, we can also define a tangent object with negatives as follows.

Definition 4.6
.

A tangent object
with negatives
in a
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
is an object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbb{K}}
together with a tangent structure with negatives
\mathbb{T}
, which consists of a
Leung monoidal functor with negatives
, which is a strict monoidal functor
\mathrm{L}^{-}[\mathbb{T}]\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}^{-}\to\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
, preserving foundational pullbacks and the universality of the vertical lift as in Definition
4.3
.

Using a similar strategy to the one used by Leung to classify tangent structure on a given category, we can unwrap Definitions
4.3
and
4.6
to have a more concrete understanding of these notions. Let us start by introducing a useful concept.

Definition 4.7
.

An
additive bundle object
over an object
\mathbb{X}
of a
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
is an additive bundle in the category
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
of endomorphisms of
\mathbb{X}
. Concretely, it consists of an object
\mathbb{X}\in{\mathbb{K}}
, two
1
-endomorphisms
B\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
and
E\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
, together with a
2
-morphism
q\colon E\Rightarrow B
, called the
projection
, a
2
-morphism
z_{q}\colon B\Rightarrow E
called the
zero morphism
, and a
2
-morphism
s_{q}\colon E_{2}\Rightarrow E
, called the
sum morphism
, satisfying the following properties:

\star

z_{q}
is a section of
q
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}}

\star

n
-fold pullbacks: for any positive integer
n
, the
n
-fold pullback of the projection
q
along itself exists in the category
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
of endomorphisms of
\mathbb{X}
, is a pointwise limit, and is preserved by each
E^{m}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=E\circ\dots\circ E
, for every positive integer
m
. The
k
-th projection
\pi_{k}\colon E_{n}\Rightarrow E
is denoted by
\pi_{k}
;

\star

s_{q}
is a bundle morphism:

Notation 4.8
.

In the following, given two

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}

A tangent object
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in
{\mathbb{K}}
is an object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbb{K}}
equipped with the following data:

tangent
1
-morphism.

A
1

;

, where

denotes the pullback of

along iself;

vertical lift.

;

A

satisfying the following properties:

\star

is an additive bundle object of
{\mathbb{K}}
;

\star

\displaystyle(z,l)\colon(\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}},\mathrm{T},p,z,s)\to(\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}^{2},\mathrm{T}p,\mathrm{T}z,\mathrm{T}s)

is a morphism of additive bundle objects;

\star

Additivity of canonical flip:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}},c)\colon(\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}^{2},\mathrm{T}p,\mathrm{T}z,\mathrm{T}s)\to(\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}^{2},p_{\mathrm{T}},z_{\mathrm{T}},s_{\mathrm{T}})

is a morphism of additive bundle objects;

\star

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l\mathrm{T}}

\star

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c\mathrm{T}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}

Universality of the vertical lift. The following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}}}
