is a pointwise pullback diagram, where:

\displaystyle\phi\colon\mathrm{T}_{2}A\xrightarrow{l\times_{A}z_{\mathrm{T}}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}_{2}A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}s}\mathrm{T}^{2}A

We refer to the tuple
\mathrm{T}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=(\mathrm{T},p,z,s,l,c)
as a
tangent structure
over
\mathbb{X}
. Finally, a tangent object with negatives is a tangent object equipped with an extra structure:

negation.

A
2
-morphism
n\colon\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}
, called
negation
, such that:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathsf{id},n\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

We introduce the following naming convention.

Notation 4.9
.

Given a
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
whose objects are called with a name "
x
", we refer to a tangent object of
{\mathbb{K}}
as a
tangent
x
.

The next example shows that our naming convention is consistent with the notion of tangent category, that is, tangent categories are tangent objects in the
2
-category of categories.

Example 4.10
.

The obvious example of tangent objects is given by tangent categories. Thanks to Leung’s classification theorem, a tangent category is a category
\mathbb{X}
equipped with a strict monoidal functor
\mathrm{L}[\mathbb{T}]\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\to\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
satisfying some universality conditions. So, by taking the
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of (small) categories, functors and natural transformations, we see that a tangent object of
\mathsf{CAT}
is precisely a tangent category.

Notice that, as pointed out by Lucyshyn-Wright (see Remark
4.2
), for tangent objects of
\mathsf{CAT}
to be tangent categories, it is important that the limit diagrams involved in Definition
4.3
are pointwise.

Example 4.11
.

Let
{\mathbb{K}}
be a
2
-category and consider the
2
-category
\mathsf{MND}({\mathbb{K}})
whose objects are pairs
(\mathbb{X},S)
formed by an object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbb{K}}
and a monad
S
of
\mathbb{X}
. Recall that a monad in a
2
-category over an object
\mathbb{X}
consists of a monoid in the monoidal category
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
of endomorphisms of
\mathbb{X}
. Concretely, a monad consists of an endomorphism
S\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
together with two
2
-morphisms
\eta\colon\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\Rightarrow S
and
\gamma\colon S^{2}\Rightarrow S
, where
S^{2}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=S\circ S
, satisfying associativity and unitality conditions. A morphism of monads
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},S)\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},S^{\prime})
consists of a
1
-morphism
F\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
together with a
2
-morphism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

satisfying some compatibilities with the units
\eta
and
\eta^{\prime}
and the multiplications
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
of the monads
S
and
S^{\prime}
(see
[
Str72
]
for details). A
2
-morphism
\theta\colon(F,\alpha)\Rightarrow(G,\beta)
of
\mathsf{MND}({\mathbb{K}})
between two
1
-morphisms
(F,\alpha),(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
consists of a
2
-morphism
\theta\colon F\Rightarrow G
, compatible with the distributive laws.

By spelling out the details one finds out that a tangent object of
\mathsf{MND}({\mathbb{K}})
consists of a tangent object
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbb{K}}
together with a monad
S
of
\mathbb{X}
equipped with a
2
-morphism
\alpha\colon S\circ\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}\circ S
compatible with the tangent structure
\mathbb{T}
of
\mathbb{X}
. We refer to
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};S,\alpha)
as a
tangent monad
in
{\mathbb{K}}
. When
{\mathbb{K}}
is the
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
, tangent monads in
{\mathbb{K}}
are precisely tangent monads, as introduced by Cockett, Lemay, and Lucyshyn-Wright in
[
CLL20
]
.

Example 4.12
.

Let us consider the
2
-category
\mathsf{MONCAT}
whose objects are monoidal categories
(\mathbb{X},\otimes,\mathds{1},\alpha,\lambda,\rho)
with associator
\alpha
and left and right unitors
\lambda
and
\rho
, respectively,
1
-morphisms are strong monoidal functors
(F,\varepsilon,\mu)\colon(\mathbb{X},\otimes,\mathds{1},\alpha,\lambda,\rho)\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\otimes^{\prime},\mathds{1}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
, i.e., functors
F\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
together with an isomorphism
\varepsilon\colon\mathds{1}^{\prime}\to F\mathds{1}
and a natural isomorphism
\mu_{X,Y}\colon F(X)\otimes^{\prime}F(Y)\to F(X\otimes Y)
, compatible with the associators and the unitors, and
2
-morphisms are natural transformations compatible with the morphisms
\varepsilon
and
\mu
of the strong monoidal functors.

A tangent object of
\mathsf{MONCAT}
consists of a monoidal category
(\mathbb{X},\otimes,\mathds{1},\alpha,\lambda,\rho)
equipped with a tangent structure, so that
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is also a tangent category and with an isomorphism
\mathds{1}\to\mathrm{T}(\mathds{1})
, that we call
tangent unitor
, and a natural isomorphism
\mathrm{T}M\otimes\mathrm{T}N\to\mathrm{T}(M\otimes N)
that we call
tangent distributor
, compatible with the associator and the unitors. Employing the Convention
4.9
, we call the tangent objects of
\mathsf{MONCAT}
,
tangent monoidal categories
.

Notice that the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGCAT}
of tangent categories admits products. This allows one to wonder what are pseudomonoids of
\mathsf{TNGCAT}
. Recall that a pseudomonoid in a
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
with products consists of an object
\mathbb{X}
, together with two
1
-morphisms
\otimes\colon\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
and
\eta\colon\mathds{1}\to\mathbb{X}
,
\mathds{1}
being terminal in
{\mathbb{K}}
, and three
2
-isomorphisms:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes\times\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\times\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{1}\times\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta\times\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle!,\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\times\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}},!\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

satisfying the same pentagonal and hexagonal diagrams of the associator and unitors in the definition of a monoidal category.

As tangent objects in the
2
-category of monads are equivalent to monads in the
2
-category of tangent objects, tangent monoidal categories are equivalent to pseudomonoids in the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGCAT}
of tangent categories. We refer to this second description as
monoidal tangent categories
and in the future we use the terms tangent monoidal categories and monoidal tangent categories, interchangeably.

Proposition 4.13
.

There is an equivalence between the category of tangent monoidal categories and the category of monoidal tangent categories, defined as pseudomonoids in the category of tangent categories.

Example
4.12
can be extended to other classes of monoidal categories. For example, one can consider braided monoidal categories, symmetric monoidal categories, or closed symmetric monoidal categories. The corresponding tangent objects are then called
tangent braided monoidal categories
,
tangent symmetric monoidal categories
, and
tangent closed symmetric monoidal categories
.

Example 4.14
.

An enriched category
\mathbb{Y}
over a monoidal category
(\mathbb{X},\otimes,\mathds{1},\alpha,\lambda,\rho)
consists of a collection of objects together, for each pair
M,N
of objects, an object
\mathbb{Y}(M,N)
of
\mathbb{X}
, which plays the role of the Hom-Set functor (cf.
[
Kel05
]
). Moreover, an enriched category comes equipped with a collection of morphisms of
\mathbb{X}
:

\displaystyle\circ\colon\mathbb{Y}(N,P)\otimes\mathbb{Y}(M,N)\to\mathbb{Y}(M,P)

which plays the role of the composition of morphisms, and a collection of morphisms of
\mathbb{X}
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{id}\colon\mathds{1}\to\mathbb{Y}(M,M)

which plays the role of the identity morphisms. One can define a
2
-category
\mathsf{ENRCH}
whose objects are pairs
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})
formed by a monoidal category
\mathbb{X}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=(\mathbb{X},\otimes,\mathds{1},\alpha,\lambda,\rho)
together with an enriched category
\mathbb{Y}
over
\mathbb{X}
. A
1
-morphism
(F,G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Y}^{\prime})
of
\mathsf{ENRCH}
consists of a strong monoidal functor
F\colon\kern-4.30554pt=(F,\varepsilon,\eta)\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
together with a pair
(G,\beta)\colon\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{Y}^{\prime}
, formed by an assignment
G
which sends an object
M
of
\mathbb{Y}
to an object
GM
of
\mathbb{Y}^{\prime}
, together with a collection of isomorphisms of
\mathbb{X}
:

\beta\colon F(\mathbb{Y}(M,N))\to\mathbb{Y}^{\prime}(GM,GN)

compatible with the morphisms
\circ
and
\mathsf{id}
. Finally,
2
-morphisms
(\varphi,\psi)\colon(F,G,\beta)\to(F^{\prime},G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
between two
1
-morphisms
(F,G,\beta),(F^{\prime},G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Y}^{\prime})
consist of a natural transformation of strong monoidal functors
\varphi\colon F\to F^{\prime}
together with a collection of morphisms:

\displaystyle\psi\colon\mathbb{Y}^{\prime}(GM,GN)\to\mathbb{Y}^{\prime}(G^{\prime}M,G^{\prime}N)

satisfying the following condition:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathbb{Y}(M,N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\prime}\mathbb{Y}(M,N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{Y}^{\prime}(GM,GN)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{Y}^{\prime}(G^{\prime}M,G^{\prime}N)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

Spelling out the details one finds that a tangent object of
\mathsf{ENRCH}
consists of tangent monoidal category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
together with an
\mathbb{X}
-enriched category
\mathbb{Y}
equipped with an assignment
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
which sends an object
M
to another object
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}M
of
\mathbb{Y}
, with a collection of isomorphisms
\beta\colon\mathrm{T}(\mathbb{Y}(M,N))\to\mathbb{Y}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}M,\mathrm{T}^{\prime}N)
, compatible with
\circ
and
\mathsf{id}
. Moreover,
\mathbb{Y}
comes equipped with a list of collections of morphisms of
\mathbb{X}
,
p^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{Y}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}M,\mathrm{T}^{\prime}N)\to\mathbb{Y}(M,N)
,
z^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{Y}(M,N)\to(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}M,\mathrm{T}^{\prime}N)
,
s^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{Y}_{2}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}M,\mathrm{T}^{\prime}N)\to\mathbb{Y}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}M,\mathrm{T}^{\prime}N)
,
\mathbb{Y}_{2}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}M,\mathrm{T}^{\prime}N)
denoting the pullback of
p^{\prime}
along itself,
l^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{Y}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}M,\mathrm{T}^{\prime}N)\to\mathbb{Y}({\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}M,{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}N)
, and
c\colon\mathbb{Y}({\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}M,{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}N)\to\mathbb{Y}({\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}M,{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}N)
, satisfying the compatibility conditions required by the axioms of a tangent structure of
\mathbb{X}
.

In future work, we intend to explore notions like tangent model categories, tangent internal categories, double tangent categories (that are tangent objects in the
2
-category of tangent categories), tangent double categories (that are tangent objects in the
2
-category of double categories), tangent topoi, tangent sheaves and many others.

The next step is to introduce
1
-morphisms of tangent objects.

Definition 4.15
.

A
lax
1
-morphism of tangent objects

(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
between two tangent objects
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
consists of a
1
-morphism
F\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
of
{\mathbb{K}}
together with a
2
-morphism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

compatible with the tangent structures as follows:

\star

Additivity:
(\mathsf{id}_{F},\alpha)\colon(F,F\circ\mathrm{T},Fp,Fz,Fs)\to(F,\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F,p^{\prime}_{F},z^{\prime}_{F},s^{\prime}_{F})
is an additive bundle morphism;

\star

Compatibility with the vertical lifts:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fl}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l^{\prime}F}

\star

Compatibility with the canonical flips:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}\circ F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fc}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\alpha}

Similarly, a
colax
1
-morphism of tangent objects

(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\nrightarrow(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
consists of a
1
-morphism
G\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
together with a
2
-morphism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

satisfying the dual conditions of
\alpha
. Moreover, the functor:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{X},G)\colon{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X})\to{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}^{\prime})

is required to preserve the universality of the
n
-fold pointwise pullback of the projection along itself and the pointwise pullback of the universal property of the vertical lift.

A colax morphism
(F,\alpha)
of tangent objects is
strong
if
\alpha
is invertible and
strict
if
\alpha
is the identity.

We are also interested in defining
2
-morphisms of tangent objects.

Definition 4.16
.

A
lax
2
-morphism of tangent objects

\theta\colon(F,\alpha)\to(G,\beta)
between two
1
-morphisms
(F,\alpha),(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
consists of a
2
-morphism of
{\mathbb{K}}

\theta\colon F\Rightarrow G
, satisfying the following condition:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

Similarly, a
colax
2
-morphism of tangent objects

\theta\colon(F,\alpha)\to(G,\beta)
between two
1
-morphisms
(F,\alpha),\linebreak(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\nrightarrow(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
consists of a
2
-morphism of
{\mathbb{K}}

\theta\colon F\Rightarrow G
, satisfying the dual conditions of a lax
2
-morphism. Finally, a
double morphism of tangent objects

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\beta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\shortmid}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\shortmid}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})}

for the lax
1
-morphisms
(F_{\scriptscriptstyle\square},\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle\square})
and
(F_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare},\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare})
, and the colax
1
-morphisms
(G,\beta)
and
(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
, is a
2
-morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

satisfying the following condition:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{\prime}\circ F_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}\circ\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}\circ F_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}\circ G^{\prime}\circ F_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\circ G\circ\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\circ\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}^{\prime}\circ G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}\circ F_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\circ G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\prime}F_{\scriptscriptstyle\square}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}^{\prime}\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare}G}

Notation 4.17
.

Tangent objects of a
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
together with lax tangent
1
-morphisms and lax
2
-morphisms form a
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbb{K}})
. Similarly, tangent objects of
{\mathbb{K}}
together with colax tangent
1
-morphisms and colax
2
-morphisms form a
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}_{\mathsf{co}}({\mathbb{K}})
. The
2
-subcategory of
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbb{K}})
whose
1
-morphisms are strong, that is the distributive law is invertible, is denoted by
\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbb{K}})
and the
2
-subcategory of
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbb{K}})
whose
1
-morphisms are strict, i.e., the distributive law is the identity, is denoted by
\mathsf{TNG}_{=}({\mathbb{K}})
. Finally, tangent objects together with lax tangent
1
-morphisms as horizontal morphisms, colax tangent
1
-morphisms as vertical morphisms, and double tangent cells for double cells form also a double category denoted by
\mathbb{T}\mathsf{NG}({\mathbb{K}})
.

When
{\mathbb{K}}
is the
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories, the double category
\mathbb{T}\mathsf{NG}(\mathsf{CAT})
is precisely the double category
\mathbb{T}\mathsf{NGCAT}
of tangent categories, first described in
[
Lan24
, Proposition 2.2]
.

We conclude this section, by showing that the assignment
\mathsf{TNG}
which sends a
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
to the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbb{K}})
of tangent objects of
{\mathbb{K}}
extends to a
2
-functor. For this purpose, we first need to select the correct class of morphisms between
2
-categories. Indeed, if
F\colon{\mathbb{K}}\to{\mathbb{K}}^{\prime}
is an arbitrary
2
-functor and
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
a tangent object of
{\mathbb{K}}
, in general, there is no reason to believe that
F\mathbb{X}
is also a tangent object of
{\mathbb{K}}^{\prime}
.

Indeed, in order to make
F\mathbb{X}
into a tangent object, the
2
-functor
F
must preserve the
n
-fold pullbacks of the projection with itself and the universality of the vertical lift.

Recall that a
2
-functor
F\colon{\mathbb{K}}\to{\mathbb{K}}^{\prime}
(notice that here we work with strict
2
-functors) is an assignment which sends objects
M
,
1
-morphisms
f\colon M\to N
, and
2
-morphisms
\theta\colon f\Rightarrow g
of
{\mathbb{K}}
to objects
F_{0}M
,
1
-morphisms
F_{1}f\colon F_{0}M\to F_{0}N
, and
2
-morphisms
F_{2}\theta\colon F_{1}f\Rightarrow F_{2}g
, respectively, in a compatible way with the composition and the identities.

Definition 4.18
.

A
2
-functor
F\colon{\mathbb{K}}\to{\mathbb{K}}^{\prime}
is
2
-pullback preserving
if it preserves pullbacks of type

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

in each category
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
, for each object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbb{K}}
.

If
F
is a
2
-pullback preserving
2
-functor and
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
a tangent object of
{\mathbb{K}}
, it is not hard to see that,
F\mathbb{X}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=F_{0}\mathbb{X}
comes equipped with a tangent structure so defined:

tangent bundle morphism.

The tangent bundle morphism
F\mathrm{T}\colon F\mathbb{X}\to F\mathbb{X}
is given by:

\displaystyle F_{1}\mathrm{T}\colon F_{0}\mathbb{X}\to F_{0}\mathbb{X}

projection.

The projection
Fp\colon F\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow\mathsf{id}_{F\mathbb{X}}
is given by:

\displaystyle F_{2}p\colon F_{1}\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow F_{1}\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}=\mathsf{id}_{F_{0}\mathbb{X}}

zero morphism.

The zero morphism
Fz\colon\mathsf{id}_{F\mathbb{X}}\Rightarrow F\mathrm{T}
is given by:

\displaystyle F_{2}z\colon\mathsf{id}_{F_{0}\mathbb{X}}=F_{1}\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\Rightarrow F_{1}\mathrm{T}

sum morphism.

The sum morphism
Fs\colon(F\mathrm{T})_{2}\Rightarrow F\mathrm{T}
is given by:

\displaystyle(F_{1}\mathrm{T})_{2}\cong F_{1}(\mathrm{T}_{2})\xRightarrow{F_{2}s}F_{1}\mathrm{T}

vertical lift.

The vertical lift
Fl\colon F\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow(F\mathrm{T})^{2}
is given by:

\displaystyle F_{2}l\colon F_{1}\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow F_{1}(\mathrm{T}^{2})=(F_{1}\mathrm{T})^{2}

canonical flip.

The canonical flip
Fc\colon(F\mathrm{T})^{2}\Rightarrow(F\mathrm{T})^{2}
is given by:

\displaystyle F_{2}c\colon(F_{1}\mathrm{T})^{2}=F_{1}(\mathrm{T}^{2})\Rightarrow F_{1}(\mathrm{T}^{2})=(F_{1}\mathrm{T})^{2}

Moreover, if
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
has negatives with negation
n
, then:

negation.

The negation
Fn\colon F\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow F\mathrm{T}
is given by:

\displaystyle F_{2}n\colon F_{1}\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow F_{1}\mathrm{T}

Remark 4.19
.

Note that the pullback preservation property is only a sufficient condition for a
2
-functor to preserve tangent objects. Indeed, one can ask for stricter conditions on
2
-functors. For the sake of simplicity, we decided to adopt the weaker condition expressed by Definition
4.18
.

Notation 4.20
.

2
-categories,
2
-functors, and
2
-natural transformations form a
2
-category denoted by
2\mathsf{CAT}
. Moreover, the composition of two
2
-pullback preserving
2
-functors is still a
2
-pullback preserving
2
-functor. Thus, also
2
-categories,
2
-pullback preserving
2
-functors and
2
-natural transformations form a
2
-category that will be denoted by
2\mathsf{CAT}_{\mathsf{pp}}
.

Proposition 4.21
.

The assignment
\mathsf{TNG}
which sends a
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
to the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbb{K}})
of tangent objects of
{\mathbb{K}}
extends to a
2
-functor
\mathsf{TNG}\colon 2\mathsf{CAT}_{\mathsf{pp}}\to 2\mathsf{CAT}
. Similarly, also
\mathsf{TNG}_{\mathsf{co}}
,
\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}
, and
\mathsf{TNG}_{=}
extend to
2
-functors
2\mathsf{CAT}_{\mathsf{pp}}\to 2\mathsf{CAT}
.

5
The Grothendieck construction in the context of tangent categories

In Section
3.2
we presented an adjunction between tangent fibrations and indexed tangent categories. However, this adjunction is not an equivalence of categories.

To find the correct notion, we employ the concept of a tangent object, introduced in Section
4
. First, we show that tangent objects in the
2
-category
\mathsf{FIB}
of fibrations are precisely tangent fibrations.

Proposition 5.1
.

The following are equivalences of
2
-categories:

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNG}(\mathsf{FIB})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathsf{TNGFIB}

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNG}_{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{FIB})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}(\mathsf{FIB})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\cong}

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNG}_{=}(\mathsf{FIB})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{=}

Remark 5.2
.

Notice that we do not require the base tangent morphism of a
1
-morphim in
\mathsf{TNGFIB}
to be strict anymore (see Remark
3.20
), as required in Definition
3.16
. This was employed to simplify the definition of the functor
\mathfrak{I}
in the reduced Grothendieck construction. Now, there is no more need for this technical assumption. Notice also that, if one prefers base tangent morphisms to be strict, one can simply consider
2
-morphisms
(\theta;\theta^{\prime})
in
\mathsf{FIB}
where the base natural transformation
\theta
is the identity. Then,
1
-morphisms of tangent objects in this
2
-category are precisely pairs of tangent morphisms whose base morphism is strict.

The goal is to compare tangent fibrations with some notion of indexed categories. Thanks to Proposition
5.1
, we now know that tangent fibrations are tangent objects in
\mathsf{FIB}
. Moreover, the Grothendieck construction defines an equivalence between fibrations and indexed categories. Therefore, we can apply the
2
-functor
\mathsf{TNG}
to this equivalence.

Definition 5.3
.

A
tangent indexed category
is a tangent object in the
2
-category
\mathsf{INDX}
of indexed categories. Moreover, a tangent indexed category
with negatives
is a tangent object with negatives in the
2
-category
\mathsf{INDX}
.

Remark 5.4
.

A tangent indexed category should not be confused with an indexed tangent category, defined in Definition
3.8
. We show in a moment that these two notions are related via an adjunction, which however is not an equivalence.

Let us unpack Definition
5.3
. A tangent indexed category consists of the following data:

base tangent category.

A base tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
;

indexed category.

An indexed category
\mathfrak{I}\colon\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{CAT}
;

indexed tangent bundle functor.

A collection of functors
\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}\to\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A\right)}
, indexed by the objects
A
of
\mathbb{X}
;

tangent distributors.

A collection of natural transformations
\kappa^{\left(f\right)}
, indexed by the morphisms
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{X}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A\right)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa^{\left(f\right)}}

indexed projection.

A collection of natural transformations
p^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\Rightarrow p^{\ast}
, indexed by the objects
A
of
\mathbb{X}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A\right)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\left(A\right)}}

satisfying the following property:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\circ f^{\ast}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p^{\ast}\circ f^{\ast}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}\circ p^{\ast}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\left(A\right)}f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{I}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}p^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}

indexed zero.

A collection of natural transformations
z^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}}\Rightarrow z^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}
, indexed by the objects
A
of
\mathbb{X}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\left(A\right)}}

satisfying the following property:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\circ f^{\ast}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f^{\ast}\circ z^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{z^{\ast}\circ(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\left(A\right)}f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}z^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\ast}\kappa^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{I}^{2}}

indexed
n
-fold pullback.

For every positive integer
n
, a collection of functors
\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{n}\colon\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}\to\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}_{n}A\right)}
, indexed by the objects
A
of
\mathbb{X}
, together with a collection of natural transformations
\kappa_{n}^{\left(f\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{n}\circ f^{\ast}\Rightarrow(\mathrm{T}_{n}f)^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}_{n}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}
, indexed by the morphism
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{X}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}_{n}A^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}_{n}A\right)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T}_{n}f)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{n}^{\left(f\right)}}

and a collection of natural tranformations
\pi^{\left(A\right)}_{k}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{n}\Rightarrow\pi_{k}^{\left(*\right)}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}
, indexed by the objects
A
of
\mathbb{X}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}_{n}A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\left(\mathrm{T}_{n}A\right)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{k}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\left(A\right)}_{k}}

satisfying the following property:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}\circ f^{\ast}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T}_{n}f)^{\ast}\circ\pi_{k}^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{k}^{\left(A\right)}f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{n}^{\left(f\right)}f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{k}^{\ast}\kappa^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{I}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T}_{n}f)^{\ast}\pi_{k}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}

indexed sum morphism.

A collection of natural transformations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}^{\left(A\right)}\circ f^{\ast}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{l^{\ast}\circ(\mathrm{T}^{2}f)^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(\mathrm{T}A^{\prime}\right)}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}

If the indexed tangent category has negatives, then we also have:

indexed negation.

A collection of natural transformations
n^{\left(A\right)}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\Rightarrow n^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}
, indexed by the objects

of
\mathbb{X}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n^{\ast}}

satisfying the following property:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\circ f^{\ast}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{n^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A\right)}\circ f^{\ast}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}\circ n^{\ast}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n^{\left(A\right)}f^{\ast}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n^{\ast}\kappa^{\left(f\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{I}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}n^{\left(A^{\prime}\right)}}

Notice that all these morphisms also need to satisfy the axioms of a tangent object. We can finally state the main result of this paper.

Theorem 5.5

(Grothendieck construction in the context of tangent categories)
.

The Grothendieck correspondence between fibrations and indexed categories lifts to an equivalence between the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGFIB}
of tangent fibrations and the
2
-category
{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\mathsf{TNG}(\mathsf{INDX})
of tangent indexed categories:

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGFIB}\simeq{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}

Similarly, the
2
-categories
\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co}}
and
{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}_{\mathsf{co}}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\mathsf{TNG}_{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{INDX})
are equivalent:

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co}}\simeq{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}_{\mathsf{co}}

Moreover, these two equivalences restrict to strong and strict morphisms, yielding two other equivalences:

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\cong}\simeq{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}_{\cong}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}(\mathsf{INDX})

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{=}\simeq{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}_{=}\colon\kern-4.30554pt=\mathsf{TNG}_{=}(\mathsf{INDX})

Proof.

To prove this result we only need to apply the
2
-functor
\mathsf{TNG}
of Proposition
4.21
to the equivalence
\mathsf{FIB}\simeq\mathsf{INDX}
of Proposition
2.15
. Notice that the
2
-functors of this equivalence preserve limits, so they are
1
-morphisms in
2\mathsf{CAT}_{\mathsf{pp}}
.
∎

Remark 5.6
.

Notice that the equivalence of Theorem
5.5
preserves the base tangent category. Concretely this means that if
\Pi
is a tangent fibration, then the base of the corresponding tangent indexed category
\mathscr{I}(\Pi)
is precisely the base tangent category of
\Pi
and vice versa. This can be used to restrict the equivalence to the
2
-subcategories
\mathsf{TNGFIB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\simeq{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of tangent fibrations with
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
for base tangent category and of tangent indexed categories with
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
for index tangent category, respectively, for any tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Therefore, we also obtain the equivalences:

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGFIB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\simeq{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\simeq{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}_{\mathsf{co}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{\cong}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\simeq{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}_{\cong}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGFIB}_{=}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\simeq{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}_{=}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Notice that, to obtain any possible tangent fibration and tangent indexed category over any possible base tangent category it was crucial to work with the
2
-categories
\mathsf{FIB}
and
\mathsf{INDX}
, where the base categories are not fixed. Indeed, the tangent objects over the
2
-category
\mathsf{FIB}(\mathbb{X})
of fibrations over a fixed base category
\mathbb{X}
are tangent fibrations over the trivial tangent category over
\mathbb{X}
, i.e.,
\mathsf{TNG}(\mathsf{FIB}(\mathbb{X}))\simeq\mathsf{TNGFIB}(\mathbb{X},\mathds{1})
, where
\mathds{1}
denotes the trivial tangent structure. Similarly,
\mathsf{TNG}(\mathsf{INDX}(\mathbb{X}))\simeq{\mathsf{TNGINDX}}(\mathbb{X},\mathds{1})
.

On one hand, in Section
3.2
we proved that the correspondence between indexed tangent categories and tangent fibrations forms an adjunction which, however, is not an equivalence. On the other hand, Theorem
5.5
shows that tangent indexed categories are equivalent to tangent fibrations. This allows one to define an adjunction between indexed tangent categories and tangent indexed categories.

Informally, this adjunction can be regarded as the commutator between two operations:
\mathsf{INDX}
which makes objects into “
indexed objects
” and the
2
-functor
\mathsf{TNG}
. With this interpretation, we can see that these two operations do not commute, in contrast with other examples, like the
2
-functor
\mathsf{MND}
which sends a
2
-category
{\mathbb{K}}
to the
2
-category of monads in
{\mathbb{K}}
and the
2
-functor
\mathsf{TNG}
(see Example
4.11
).

Furthermore, Corollary
3.27
establishes that, when the base tangent category is trivial, the adjunction between tangent fibrations and indexed tangent categories on such a base tangent category becomes an equivalence. This, together with Theorem
5.5
, implies that, when the base tangent category is trivial, indexed tangent categories and tangent indexed categories are equivalent.

A similar phenomenon was observed in
[
MV20
]
by Moeller and Vasilakopoulou in the context of a Grothendieck construction for monoidal categories. In particular, they discussed two distinct notions of “monoidal indexed categories”, one that carries a global monoidal structure, and the second one whose monoidal structure is fibrewise. They showed that, when the base monoidal category is cartesian monoidal, these two notions are equivalent. This is similar to our result: when the base tangent category is trivial, the two notions of “tangent indexed categories” coincide.

6
Conclusions

In this paper, we discussed two main approaches to defining a Grothendieck construction in the context of tangent categories. In Section
3
, we recalled Cockett and Cruttwell’s result which establishes that a tangent fibration can be associated with an indexed tangent category. We partially reconstructed the tangent fibration from the corresponding indexed tangent category, by providing an adjunction of
2
-functors (Theorem
3.25
). We noticed that this adjunction does not provide a full equivalence, except when the base tangent category is trivial (Corollary
3.27
). In Section
3.3
, we also compared Street’s notion of internal fibrations in a
2
-category with the notion of tangent fibrations (Theorem
3.30
).

To sidestep this issue and find a genuine equivalence between tangent fibrations and a suitable notion of indexed categories, we introduced a new concept: the notion of tangent objects. We then employed tangent objects to lift the equivalence between fibrations and indexed categories to an equivalence between tangent fibrations and tangent indexed categories, i.e., tangent objects in the
2
-category of indexed categories (Theorem
5.5
).

In this paper, we also proved the following results:

Example
4.10
.

We showed that tangent categories are precisely the tangent objects in the
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
;

Example
4.11
.

We showed that tangent monads are precisely the tangent objects in the
2
-category
\mathsf{MND}
;

Corollary
3.27
.

We showed that when the base tangent category is trivial the reduced Grothendieck construction becomes an equivalence;

Proposition
5.1
.

We showed that tangent fibrations are precisely the tangent objects in the
2
-category
\mathsf{FIB}
.

We also introduced these new concepts:

Definition
3.8
.

We introduced the notion of an indexed tangent category;

Definition
4.3
.

We introduced the concept of a tangent object in a strict
2
-category;

Example
4.12
.

We introduced the concept of a tangent monoidal category;

Example
4.14
.

We introduced the concept of a tangent enriched category;

Definition
5.3
.

We defined the concept of a tangent indexed category.

6.1
Future work

In this paper, we introduced a new concept: the notion of tangent objects. We have already shown that tangent categories, tangent monads, and tangent fibrations are all examples of tangent objects in suitable
2
-categories and we employed tangent objects to introduce the new notions of tangent monoidal categories, tangent enriched categories, and tangent indexed categories.

Developing a formal theory of tangent objects will have important benefits in understanding all these notions. In particular, we are interested in extending notions of tangent category theory, such as vector fields, differential object, differential bundles, or connections, to the formal context of tangent objects.

We also expect the Grothendieck construction for tangent categories to play an important role in tangent category theory. In particular, in
[
CL23a
]
, Cruttwell and Lemay introduced the notion of a reverse tangent category and they employed the notion of a dual fibration. We are interested in understanding what kind of structure is carried by the dual of a tangent fibration.
